# SwarmAI -- Lessons & Patterns

_This document captures what worked, what failed, and what to watch for when developing SwarmAI. Swarm updates this automatically after significant sessions. You can also edit it directly._

## What Worked
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [guideline] **A "fix DoD3" charter was STALE — the original problem was already solved, and Gate-1 uncovered the REAL open bug: a SECOND ungated door onto the same R1 invariant (run_ca984ce5, P8 one-brain-many-doors).** DoD3 was chartered as "make reviewed_paths run-authoritative / kill the git-range rubber-stamp." I started designing Direction ② (defer per-cycle commit). Gate-1 (verified against source) proved TWO things: (1) **the git-range rubber-stamp was ALREADY reverted** (run_c526d393 FIX A) — `_reviewed_paths_at_head` (runtime_hooks.py:1017) already uses the safe working-tree `git diff --name-only HEAD`, there is NO live range in the gate; I was about to "fix" an already-fixed problem, and Direction ② would have made things *worse* (deferring commit enlarges the un-reviewed working-tree window). (2) the ACTUAL open hole: the session-close `auto_commit_hook._smart_commit` commits via `subprocess.run(["git","commit"])`, **BYPASSING** `create_adversarial_commit_gate` (which only intercepts the Bash-tool `git commit` path, security_hooks.py:2187) — so an un-reviewed CODE edit in the SwarmWS repo auto-commits with no adversarial marker. **Durable tells:** (a) **before designing a fix for a chartered task, VERIFY the charter's premise against current source — a task can be stale because a prior run already fixed it;** the reflex to start designing the moment the task is named is exactly how you "fix" a solved problem and entrench worse behavior (P9: does this thing still need doing?). (b) **P8 in practice: an invariant enforced at ONE door (Bash `git commit` gate) is only as strong as its WEAKEST door — find ALL the write paths onto the same invariant.** The subprocess commit was a second door onto R1 that no prior run enumerated; a gate on door 1 with door 2 wide open enforces nothing against a determined-or-accidental door-2 write. (c) **premise correction mid-run:** I initially framed the hole as "un-reviewed `backend/` source" — Gate-1 corrected it: auto_commit commits **SwarmWS** (a SEPARATE git repo from the swarmai source repo), so it can't touch `backend/`; the real surface is the 99 in-SwarmWS tracked code files (pipeline engine, pollinate services). Never assume which repo a hook writes to — `git rev-parse --show-toplevel` it. The fix (Option B) reuses the SAME coverage markers as the Bash gate (identical fail-open cases) to withhold un-reviewed CODE, scoped to marker-present sessions; a marker-less chat session stays a documented narrow gap. Gate-2 then caught a MED (`.mjs` tracked-code missed by the extension set — my whitelist-trap class, running in the *under-inclusive* direction). Honest read: the stale-charter catch AND the premise correction were both Gate-1's (same model, refute seat), not my in-the-moment judgment. (2026-08-11, run_ca984ce5)
- [guideline] **An identity comparison across two SAME-LOOKING-but-DIFFERENT session-id namespaces is a silent security-gate false-positive — Gate-2 caught a HIGH I'd have shipped (run_a8bcc469, DoD1 history-rewrite guardrail).** Building a gate that blocks `git rebase`/`reset --hard`/`branch -f`/etc. ONLY when a *sibling* live session exists, I wrote `_other_live_sessions_present(session_key)` comparing the session registry's `_units` keys against `session_key`. Both are "the session id" by name — but they live in **different namespaces**: `_units` is keyed by the **app `session_id`** (`session_router.py:1452`, also the value stored in `session_context["sdk_session_id"]` at `:2436`), while `session_key = resume_session_id or agent_id` (`hook_builder.py:368`) is the SDK/agent id (often the literal `"default"`). Result: a truly SOLO session's own unit never self-matched → `_other_live_sessions_present` returned True → **every solo history-rewrite was falsely gated (auto-DENIED in headless)** — the exact opposite of the "solo → approve" design. The sibling hook `auto_commit._other_live_sessions_touched` got it right (compares the same namespace); my new fn's docstring even *claimed* "same source" while using the wrong key. **Durable tells:** (a) **when two variables are both "the X id", never assume they're the same value — trace each to where it's PRODUCED/KEYED and confirm the namespace matches before comparing them for identity;** a name match ("both are session ids") is not a namespace match. (b) **A security gate's false-POSITIVE (bricks legit work) is as shippable-bad as its false-negative** — my unit tests missed it because they monkeypatched the whole fn AND hand-keyed the mock registry with the same string they queried, so the real key mismatch was never exercised; the fix added a regression test that keys `_units` with the app session_id and asserts a solo session returns False. (c) Same session also surfaced a MED: an unfiltered `_units` scan counts DEAD/COLD units (retained ~1h by `_purge_stale_cold`) as "live" siblings → filter `is_alive` via `list_units()`. Honest containment read: BOTH the HIGH and MED were model-layer errors I did NOT self-catch — the Gate-2 adversarial (same model, refute seat) caught them; the gate's edge was STANCE, not intelligence. The paired DoD2 (add MultiEdit/NotebookEdit + Bash-write-targets to the file-tracker exclude set) shipped clean. (2026-08-11, run_a8bcc469)

- [guideline] **A git-range can NEVER prove adversarial-review COVERAGE on a commit-to-main shared repo — coverage must bind to the reviewer's own declared scope (run_c526d393, Gate-1 AND Gate-2 both BLOCKED the same wrong instinct).** Fixing the adversarial-commit gate's `reviewed_paths` (the R1 evidence that a `git commit` only lands adversarially-reviewed files), I twice reached for a git range to answer "what did the reviewer review", and both were rubber-stamps: **(1) Gate-1 killed `origin/main...HEAD`** — on a pipeline that commits directly to `main` that range is the whole-branch divergence (measured 895 files in the source repo, 8634 in SwarmWS, merge-base 2 months stale) → a reviewer of 2 files would attest coverage of thousands → the R1 subset check (`pending <= covered`) becomes trivially true. **(2) Gate-2 killed the "fix" — a run-scoped `git log HEAD --since=run.created_at --name-only` window** — still has NO pathspec, so it unions EVERY file touched on HEAD in the run window (measured 1350 files / 226 commits over 3 days in SwarmWS, incl. parallel sessions' commits) into `reviewed_paths`. Same rubber-stamp, re-expressed as a time filter instead of a branch range. **The durable lesson: `--since`/`origin/main...HEAD`/any git range is a filter over what LANDED, not a proof of what was REVIEWED; on a shared commit-to-main repo the two diverge by orders of magnitude. Review-coverage evidence must come from the reviewer's declared scope (the paths the sub-agent was ASKED to review / this run's own `files_touched`), never a repo/time window.** The disciplined outcome was to REVERT the whole `reviewed_paths` change (FIX A) rather than force a narrower-but-still-broken window through, and ship only the independent completion-gate fix (FIX B) — scope reduced under adversarial pressure, not a broken half forced through. Honest containment read: BOTH catches were the gates (same model, refute seat), not my in-the-moment judgment — I proposed the git-range twice; the gate's edge was STANCE (refute-this-diff) not intelligence. **FIX B (the half that shipped) also dog-fooded itself live:** `cmd_run_commit`'s already-committed branch now credits the real per-cycle commit into `commits[]` (bounded by per-file pathspec + reachable-from-HEAD + `--since=created_at`, fail-closed on empty since — a per-file pathspec is safe for a *liveness* gate even though a bare window is unsafe for a *security* gate), so this very run reached COMPLETE + committed with zero FORCE / git-surgery / hand-editing — the exact BUILD:428-vs-completion-gate contradiction it fixes. (2026-08-11, run_c526d393)

- [guideline] **DDD detail card → user-judgment language (run_d1e933aa): the Gate-0 skeptic REVERSED my framing TWICE with source evidence, before any code — and both reversals killed a Principle-1-violating "honest-looking" metric.** Asked to make the detail card show "is this DDD healthy / drifted / valuable / big", I designed a 4-question card. Gate-0 (a zero-context skeptic on the SAME model, run from the refute seat) read the actual backend and overturned two of my signals: **(1) "drift" as a composite of staleness+contradiction+decay was FABRICATED PRECISION** — `contradiction` is a hardcoded constant 50 (`ddd_health.py:318`, never computed) and base-`decay` is also a 50 placeholder whose "real" value is a self-referential delta-of-composite; only `staleness` is a real signal, so blending three into one "drift" number manufactures a metric from 1.5 real inputs. **(2) the trust "health" badge had NO scheduled writer** — `section_health.json` was only written on-demand by a `GET /engine-metrics` hit, no job/hook refreshed it, so the score froze stale forever (the exact R30#4 / Principle-1 drift the card was meant to surface health WITH). Both were "honest-looking" numbers that were actually dishonest. **Durable tells:** (a) **before surfacing ANY composite/score as a headline judgment signal, trace each input to where it's PRODUCED — a constant placeholder or a self-referential delta is fabricated precision, not a signal;** (b) **a metric with no scheduled writer is a frozen lie — verify the refresh path exists (a job/hook actually recomputes it), don't assume "the value is there" means "the value is live";** (c) the fix for (2) was a root fix per user's Scheme-A call — make `ddd-refresh` the SOLE scheduled writer, running `compute_section_health` UNCONDITIONALLY per project BEFORE the staleness `continue` (health ≠ doc-staleness), per-project try/except so one brain's failure doesn't abort the batch; (d) **"value ≠ size" made concrete:** rejected entry-count/"size" (Principle-1: a bigger brain isn't better) AND `last_referenced`/`ref_count` (a verified DEAD engine input, `ddd_entry_lifecycle.py:821/1113`) — replaced with `recentActivity` (30d changelog count = "is it being actively cultivated"), which counts ONLY real-timestamped in-window rows (adversarial Point-1: undated legacy rows would inflate a "30d" number → honest under-count beats dishonest inflation), and renders "—" when unknown, never a confident 0; (e) **the 7-type×3-layer bar needed a DATA-PLUMBING fix Gate-1 caught pre-code** — `entries[]` live on `detail.sections[].entries`, NOT on the `DetailHealth` the card received, so BOTH consumers rendered an empty bar; fix = consumers aggregate via `aggregateTypeCounts(sections)` and pass `typeCounts` down (DddCard never touches sections), labeled honestly "知识文档类型分布" (covers ② canonical docs, not "the whole brain"). Honest read: the two reversals were model-layer framing errors I did NOT self-catch — the skeptic (my own model, refute seat) caught them; containment held via the gate, and the gate's edge was STANCE (refute) not intelligence. (2026-08-05, run_d1e933aa)

- [guideline] **EVALUATE writing a REPRODUCTION test empirically FALSIFIED my confident root-cause TWICE before code — saving me from fixing the wrong layer (run_26aa6caa).** For the P0 Canvas tab-bleed, XG explicitly asked me to reproduce before diagnosing. I had TWO confident hypotheses, both wrong, both killed by a real test in EVALUATE: **(1)** "the git full-tree turn-end sweep is the root cause" — FALSE (that sweep had already been DELETED in a prior commit; the current emit is event-driven; I was reading stale code). **(2)** "two undefined-session tabs share a `''` storage key and bleed" — FALSE (`''` is falsy → `if(!id) return` → the `''`-key rail never even registers a listener). The test that PASSED revealed the ACTUAL mechanism: an event whose stamp is `undefined` (not `''`) sails through the consumers' fail-OPEN filter into every mounted rail. **Durable tell: a "reproduction" that PASSES is as valuable as one that fails — a falsified hypothesis in EVALUATE costs one test; the same wrong hypothesis discovered in REVIEW costs a wrong-layer fix + re-work. When I have a confident causal story for a runtime bug, the cheapest next action is a test that would go RED if the story is true, not code that assumes it (R16b: observe before asserting; C040 stale-code trap: grep the CURRENT code, a story built on a deleted mechanism is fiction).** (2026-08-05, run_26aa6caa)

- [guideline] **DddCard unify (run_6924b463) — unifying consumers with ASYMMETRIC data needs a CONDITIONAL component, not a uniform one** (2026-08-04): merged BrainHub's `BrainCard` (gallery) + `HealthStrip` (detail) into one density-driven `DddCard`, then added a `BrainHomeView` landing layer — a 3rd consumer. The trap: my first `full` density assumed every full card shows presence+lifecycle+tiles, but the **detail** consumer's data (`BrainDetail`) has NO `lifecycleStage`/cheap-health — only `DetailHealth` — while the **Home-hero** consumer (a `BrainSummary` + the hero's `BrainDetail`) has both. A uniform `full` shape matched NEITHER. Fix: each summary sub-block (header/presence/lifecycle/cheap) renders iff its prop is non-null; detail passes metrics-only → bare tiles+diagnostics (faithful HealthStrip replacement), hero passes everything → rich card. **Durable tell: before unifying N call sites into one component, diff the DATA each consumer actually carries — a shared component whose props assume data one consumer lacks is a latent half-render bug (the skeptic caught this as Gate-1 #5, pre-code).** Two more tells from the same run: **(a)** Two adversarial findings were root-fixed PRE-COMMIT, not deferred to "cycle N+1" — the diagnostics-row silent drop (cycle 2 #4 = real `DetailHealth` info loss) and the calm-card click opening the generic hub instead of the clicked brain (cycle 3 #1). The deep-link fix REUSED BrainHub's existing `openBrain` via a `swarm:show-brain-hub {detail.brain}` event rather than inventing a new overlay id — anti-defer-as-evasion (C039) + smallest-correct-change. **(b)** R27 migration completeness = grep-verified: deleting `BrainCard`/`HealthStrip` required 0 live refs remaining + `brain-card-*` testids renamed to `dddcard-*` across the consumer test file, before the delete was trusted. (2026-08-04, run manual)

- [decision] **NO-GO: TabView `renderLimit` first-paint scope killed by measurement before any code** (2026-08-04): proposed a low-risk single-file perf scope — render only `messages.slice(-40)`, reveal older via the existing load-earlier path — on the assumption "opening a long chat lags because 200 messages parse markdown synchronously." **Measured it FIRST, against real data, through the real code path:** extracted the last 200 assistant messages from the two heaviest sessions in `data.db` and ran them through the SAME unified remark pipeline `MarkdownRenderer.tsx` uses. Finding that killed the scope: in the last 200 messages only ~68–80 assistant messages carry a text block (the rest are `tool_use`/`tool_result`); full synchronous parse of ALL text-blocks in the densest session was well under a "顿一下" threshold, and 40-vs-200 showed no clean signal (JIT/GC noise dominated). So markdown parse is NOT the first-paint bottleneck the scope assumed — the real mount cost, if any, is the `tool_use`/`tool_result` component trees, and no bottleneck of noticeable magnitude was reproduced. Per Gate-0, DON'T build: starting a pipeline here = a mechanism for a non-reproduced hotspot in OT01 (the #1 recurring bug zone). Re-run the measurement (extract real messages → same remark pipeline → time slice sizes) before ever reviving this. Two corollaries: **(a)** the earlier claim "per-CodeBlock hljs highlight is a first-paint cost" is WRONG — `MarkdownRenderer.tsx:501-520` defers `hljs.highlightElement` to after first paint (rAF, cancelable); first-paint JS cost is markdown parse only. **(b)** the REAL verified frontend perf hotspot is unchanged: OT01 — `TabView.tsx` runs an unmemoized three-source more-complete-wins reconcile every render (`lastAsst` tail-scan ~4× + `asstChars` reduce), O(N) per token while streaming (verified 2026-08-04); that (useMemo/fast-path, NOT touching reconcile semantics) is the confident target if/when frontend perf debt is paid — not renderLimit. Durable tell (reinforces C042/C044 measure-before-mechanism): before building ANY perf mechanism, measure the suspected bottleneck against real production data through the real code path — a plausible hotspot can be a non-hotspot. (2026-08-04, run manual)

- [guideline] **baseRef empty-diff fix (run_030dc98e) — a deferred Known Issue closed cleanly BECAUSE the prior run reserved the seam** (2026-08-04): the run_b8ea6d5c PR-review surface deferred the committed-file-empty-diff issue but THREADED the `baseRef` param slot end-to-end (event→detail→CanvasFile) even though it left it unwired. This run only had to (a) add `?ref=` to one endpoint, (b) populate `baseRef=<sha>^` at the source, (c) apply it at FileViewer — the middle of the chain already existed. Tell: when deferring a known follow-up, RESERVE the parameter slot through the whole cross-boundary chain in the deferring run — the follow-up then touches only the two ends, not another N-hop thread. Two durable specifics: **(1) `<sha>^` is the correct this-run baseline** — the commit sha (recorded post-commit) is the commit that INTRODUCED the change, so its parent is the pre-run state; a prior commit to the same file is already folded into the parent, so it doesn't bleed. **(2) A best-effort baseline endpoint must SILENTLY fall back, never 400** — an unsafe/absent/root-parent ref downgrades to HEAD (or empty→source-view), so the security allowlist (`_SAFE_GIT_REF` fullmatch, leading-dash rejected → no git-option injection on a list-form call) never surfaces an error to the user; it just yields the historical baseline. **(3) Gate-2's ruthless trace of the suspected no-op paid for itself** — the path-match condition (`tab.filePath === initialFile.filePath`) looked like it would drop baseRef (repo-relative vs absolute), but the skeptic traced that useCanvasHost resolves the path ONCE before either side sees it, so they're the same string — the fix is NOT a no-op. Verifying the doubt against source (vs assuming the gate was wrong) is the O031 discipline. (2026-08-04, run manual)

- [guideline] **Canvas OUTPUTS PR-review surface (run_b8ea6d5c) — the 3 gates each caught a DISTINCT class of error a coding-fast pass would have shipped** (2026-08-04): a cross-boundary refactor (backend classifier + orchestrator emit + new SDK-MCP tool + frontend rail/viewer + completion-gate repurpose + skill docs). Durable tells. **(1) A 4-probe parallel source trace BEFORE EVALUATE is what made the frame real.** I fanned out 4 read-only agents (afferent emit chain / classifier verdict / finish mechanics / event contract) and only then wrote the understanding — that trace is what let Gate-0 catch my hand-wavy "backend synthesizes N events at COMPLETE" (there is NO completion callback; the ONLY emitter is tool-observation-driven, so the fix had to reuse the ui_action observe-emit precedent). Tell: for a cross-boundary change, map ALL coupled hops with parallel readers first; a frame written from partial knowledge invents mechanisms that don't exist. **(2) Gate-1's highest-value catch was CALL ORDER, not logic.** My plan put the knowledge-allowlist in `needs_human_review` — but `classify_relevance` drops bookkeeping at `streaming_orchestrator.py:309` BEFORE `needs_human_review` is ever called, so that edit alone was a total NO-OP for the very `.context/MEMORY.md` files it targeted. Tell: when flipping a gate, trace the ACTUAL call order of the pipeline of gates — the earliest one wins, and an edit to a later gate is dead if an earlier one already dropped the input. **(3) The "new kind" pattern kept a dangerous gate-relaxation off the table.** The naive fix (let `source` rows through at finish) would have leaked EVERY mid-run repo edit — the exact thing suppressed. A distinct `source-final` kind (minted ONLY by the finish tool, never by the mid-run classifier) let the rail accept the finish batch while `source` stays dropped. Tell: when you need a gate to accept X-at-time-T but reject X-at-time-U, a new value discriminated by its PRODUCER beats relaxing the gate's predicate. **(4) Gate-2's MED was a real cross-tree resolution bug** — source-final rows carry a repo-relative display path (repo ≠ SwarmWS workspace) that 404s at `/workspace/file/resolve`; fixed by sending absolutePath as the click's resolve anchor. Honest read: all three gates caught model-layer errors I did NOT self-catch (wrong-frame, no-op call-order, missed consumer) — containment held via the harness, not sharper first-pass judgment. (2026-08-04, run manual)

- [guideline] **OverlayHost hardening (run_06c49540) — EVALUATE re-scoping + a gate that reversed my own descope, both paid off** (2026-08-04): a follow-up-hardening run off an architecture review. Three durable tells. **(1) Verify the review's PREMISE before building its fix (O031 + R16b).** The review's headline concern — "two overlay-id vocabularies are hand-synced, drift-prone" — was FALSE against source: `uiCommands.ts` already derives its show-* rows from `ALL_SHOW_EVENTS` (`Object.fromEntries(map)`), and `test_ui_actions.py::test_backend_allowlist_is_bound_to_leftnav_ssot` already binds the Python allowlist by exact set-equality. One `grep`/read at EVALUATE collapsed ~60% of the proposed scope. The review's "9-vs-12 asymmetry = bug" was also wrong — it's an intentional SECURITY boundary (library/settings/eval deliberately not agent-openable); deriving `ALL_SHOW_EVENTS` from all registry keys (the review's fix) would have been a regression that widened the agent ACT surface. Tell: an external review's *findings* are inputs to verify, not a work-list to execute — trace each premise to source first; the highest-value output can be "most of this is already solved." **(2) SSOT via a type-bound tuple, not a 4th list.** Gate-1 caught that a hand-written id tuple would be the very anti-pattern the run fought. Fix: `OVERLAY_IDS` (leaf module) → `OverlayId` union; `registerOverlay(spec.id: OverlayId)` compile-binds the registry to it; a bidirectional set-equality test covers the runtime reverse. The tuple is THE source, not a copy. **(3) A DoD-gated goal run + Gate-2 reversed my own wrong call.** I descoped G3 (bridge-deglobal) as "no reachable staleness" — Gate-2 constructed the real flow I missed (the delete-agent fallback effect at ChatPage:1615 has no overlay-gate → changes selectedAgentId while History is open → stale query via the non-reactive module bridge). I verified it against source (C041: don't take a sub-agent's claim on faith), it held, and the descope became a real fix (moved the ONE reactive field to OverlayContext). Tell: a "this is unreachable" descope is exactly the confident-wrong call the adversarial gate exists to catch — state the reachability claim explicitly so the gate can attack it. (2026-08-04, run manual)

- [guideline] **Overlay re-architecture (run_fdeaead8, M1–M5) — two patterns that held under a big multi-run refactor** (2026-08-04): **(1) Anti-god-component discipline (C046) applied at extract-time.** The 4 workbench overlays (ToDo/Jobs/Pipeline/Pollinate) LOOKED like "4 mirrors → 1 WorkbenchOverlay", but measuring the real vs superficial duplication showed the shared part is only the OUTER frame (a sub-header toolbar + a right-drawer shell + a fmtTs) while the views/drawer-content/forms are genuinely unique per surface. The correct dedup was **thin optional-slot primitives** (`overlayShell`: fmtTs + WorkbenchToolbar + OverlayDrawer) + one geometry host — NOT a monolithic component forcing view/form slots (that would be the `堆叠function` the re-architecture set out to kill). The real D3 win ("4 mirrors → 1") is the registry migration itself (one host renders all four), not a god-frame. Tell: before extracting a "shared component" from N look-alikes, diff by SECTION and separate the identical *frame* from the unique *content* — extract only the frame, as composable slots. **(2) Strangler-fig sequencing enforced by a real dependency.** M5 (delete the legacy trio: chatAreaBounds/navSource/useExclusiveOverlay-hook/Modal-fullscreen) was BLOCKED on M3-tail because Settings + OS Eval were the LAST live consumers of that infra (via `useLayout.activeModal` + Modal-fullscreen). Deleting-first would have broken them; the correct order was migrate-then-delete, verified zero-consumer before every cut (grep each deleted symbol for live refs → all comment-only before `git rm`). Tell: never delete legacy infra until you've PROVEN its last consumer migrated — a "looks unused" grep that finds a live modal consumer is the difference between a clean deletion and a P0. (2026-08-04, run manual)

- [guideline] **Canvas build — 6 reusable tells the adversarial gates + E2E caught pre-commit** (2026-08-02, Canvas v1-v3 + tab-scoping + Radar removal): (1) **Self-suppressing auto-behavior** — v1 auto-surface set editorOpen=true via the panel IT opened → suppressed itself forever after the first fire. Tell: an auto-open that reads a signal its own action mutates needs *provenance* (suppress only for USER-opened files: panel-open AND current≠last-auto-opened). (2) **Blanket predicate over an absolute-path root** — "any dot-segment → bookkeeping" dropped EVERY deliverable because the workspace root is `~/.swarm-ai`. Tell: filter by a *denylist of specific dirs*, never a blanket dot-match, when paths may be absolute under a dot-root. (3) **iframe sandbox** — `allow-same-origin` (copied from FilePreviewModal) blocks `<script>` → agent HTML reports render dead. Tell: for a trusted local file needing interactivity use `allow-scripts` WITHOUT `allow-same-origin` (the *combo* is the XSS-to-parent danger; opaque origin can't reach parent) + keep a browser fallback for the packaged-WebKit-blank risk (NOT verifiable pre-deploy — QA the .app). (4) **Wrong tab signal** — `sessionId` is ambiguous (real-switch vs new-tab-resolve); the stable per-tab id is `activeTabId`. Tell: for "did the tab change" use the id that exists BEFORE the session resolves. (5) **E2E combination bugs** (each piece passes, assembly breaks): collapsed+auto-surface opened a file behind the dock (fix: auto-uncollapse on new file); collapsed had no close button. Tell: after composing N independently-tested features, walk the state machine for cross-feature states none of the unit tests cover. (6) **R27 removal** — grep ALL consumers incl. the pill before deleting: `AttentionList` was shared by `AlertsPill`, deleting it would've broken the pill that REPLACED the thing being removed. (2026-08-02)

- [decision] **"别一个一个打补丁"的正解 = 先审计穷尽所有同类腐烂点,再提取共用 SSOT,一次改完全部消费者。** TECH.md Current Roster 是仓库/tier/stars 的真相源,却被抄成多份静态副本:上上轮修了 monitor.py 扫描名单从 TECH.md 派生,这轮发现 report.py 还有两份独立副本(REPO_INFO 死字典 14 仓库过期 40 天,mattpocock 写 tier2/88K 实为 tier1/165K;外加 `_compute_ddd_health` 第 3 个手写 `"| 1 |" in l` 计数器)。**方法论:XG 点破"不能一个一个补丁打"后,正确动作是先跑全目录审计 sub-agent 穷举所有硬编码副本(确认 skill 内共 3 处 + config.yaml 属独立子系统),再提取 `load_source_matrix()` 单一解析器,让 monitor(扫描)、report(Source Matrix tab)、report(DDD health)三个消费者全改吃它——而不是又给 report 单独写一份解析。** 教训:(1) **Gate-1 抓到第 3 个解析器**——原方案只删 REPO_INFO 漏了 _compute_ddd_health 手写计数,不一起改就还是补丁(删一份留一份);"根治"必须审计所有消费点。(2) 薄封装 + 现有测试当回归网 = 零风险改每日 job 核心函数;新测试只覆盖新增能力(tier3 包含、stars int 边界)。(3) `_parse_stars` strip `*`/逗号 + float 覆盖小数 K(25.5K→25500),异常→0 不抛。(4) report 吃共用解析器的空返回要 warn(否则成绕过 fail-loud 的后门)。(5) **注释是腐烂标志**:REPO_INFO 注释 `# from TECH.md — would be parsed in production` 明说该读却写死(R16b:注释是假设,代码是真相)。**backlog(移出本轮):topic_matrix 死数组不能从 hot_topics 派生——T-* 与 HT-* 两套无映射 ID,Primary Repos 又是短名,硬修=新硬编码换旧;需先补 TECH.md 全名+统一 T-* ID 集,单独立项。** E2E: report 现显示全量 39 仓库,mattpocock 88K→165K. (2026-07-19, run_b8306bd8, bugfix)

- [decision] **信号去噪的根治 = 一个统一准入门槛套住 ALL 生产路径,不是过滤某个已知噪音源。** GitHub community monitor 42% 信号是噪音(dependabot/自动release PR/eks-distro-pr-bot)。根因不是"eks 该过滤",而是 dashboard discovery 分支把"仓库陌生"当价值无条件放行。修法:`is_valuable_signal()` 分层门槛(非PR→topics非空→非bot→有活跃度)挂到全部 4 条生产路径(scan issues/disc、dashboard、hot_topics)。**Gate-1 BLOCK 最大价值:抓到我只挂 3 条、漏了 hot_topics 第4条——号称根治却留一条 bot 能进的路;"从根上"=枚举所有生产者,不是修最显眼那条。** 真实数据验出的具体教训:(1) bot 判定 `[bot]` 后缀不够,`eks-distro-pr-bot`(19%噪音)是普通 machine-user 名,需 `-bot` 结尾+标题 `Bump/Update...to latest` 模式三判;(2) PR 混入真实路径是 dashboard 的 IssueCommentEvent(url 是 `/pull/`),非 issues endpoint;(3) 门槛顺序 bot 必须排活跃度前,否则新 bot PR 从 is_new 漏;(4) `_is_new` 必须防空值,`fromisoformat("")` 会崩整个 monitor;(5) "发现新仓库"与"零噪音"本质冲突,移除 discovery 豁免后发现力 17→~1(有意取舍,发现由 github-trending 承担)。live 48→12 噪音清零。**遗留待办(LOW,非本轮):`fetch_recent_issues` 的 `since` 用 naive local time 却带 Z 后缀,时间窗偏本地 UTC 偏移——预存 bug,单独修。** (2026-07-19, run_ca49f00b, bugfix)

- [decision] **The right fix for "two hand-maintained lists drift" is to derive one from the other at runtime — but only after a skeptic confirms the two lists AREN'T meant to be independent.** The GitHub_Community engine had monitor.py scanning a hardcoded 19-repo list while TECH.md's Source Matrix (its own documented source of truth) tracked ~40 — 21 engagement targets never scanned. The user's initial framing ("merge the config.yaml signal feeds into the track list") was WRONG-FRAME: TECH.md:6 explicitly says "3 separate matrices — DO NOT MERGE THEM" (signal feeds = dep-watch, a different axis). Gate-0 skeptic caught it; real fix was `load_tracked_repos()` deriving monitor.py's scan list from the TECH.md table at runtime (19→26 repos). **Parser design lessons, all caught pre-code by the risk-probe + Gate-1:** (1) section-scope to the "### Current Roster" heading→next-heading — a naive `| N |` row-grep also matches the Hot Topics table (topic prose, not repos); (2) extract from **column-2 cell only**, not row-wide — descriptions contain embedded `[text](url)` links that leak with a row regex; (3) **URL-first** extraction survives truncated link TEXT (full name lives in the href); (4) fail-loud RuntimeError if <5 parsed (never silently scan an empty set). Gate-2 attacked the live parse (26 repos), separator/header-row skip, embedded-pipe column shift, stale-import regression — 0 real bugs. (2026-07-19, run_242c12b2, full)

- [decision] **Privacy leak in SwarmWS provisioning fixed at the right layer — template (new users) + migration-with-rm--cached (existing users) — with both adversarial gates earning their keep.** Product invariant: SwarmWS + the default `Projects/SwarmAI` sample DDD ship publicly; every user-created Project + the 5 personal `.context` files (MEMORY/USER/EVOLUTION/STEERING/TOOLS) are private and must never enter the public repo. Root fix in `swarm_workspace_manager.py`: extend `GITIGNORE_CONTENT` (new-user template) with `Projects/*` + `!Projects/SwarmAI/` + the 5 `.context` excludes; extend the existing `verify_integrity` migration to append those rules to pre-fix workspaces AND a new `_untrack_private_content()` that `git rm --cached` (index-only, disk-safe, marker-gated, fail-open, `git ls-files | filter-out SwarmAI` never a glob) untracks already-committed private content. **Gate-1 caught a CRITICAL I would have shipped: it CLAIMED `Projects/*` breaks the `!SwarmAI` re-inclusion — but a live /tmp git experiment REFUTED the skeptic (Projects/* globs child ENTRIES so the negation IS honored; the skeptic-recommended `Projects/` form actually IGNORES SwarmAI). Verifying the skeptic's claim against reality, not obeying it, was the win (C041: a sub-agent claim is an input to verify, not a conclusion).** **Gate-2 caught a REAL CRITICAL: `if entry not in content` is a SUBSTRING check — a privacy rule mentioned in a `.gitignore` COMMENT would false-satisfy it and the real rule would never append → private files stay committable. Fixed to line-level match against stripped non-comment lines; regression test mutation-verified RED-on-revert.** All fixes mutation-tested. (2026-07-19, run_6be16622, bugfix)

- [guideline] Verify-first is a TWO-WAY gate on a "fix all findings" sweep — a bug report can be WRONG-FRAME (fixing it is negative work) just as often as real. Of 8 audit findings, Gate-0 skeptics closed 3 as verified-non-bug BEFORE any code: A3 "double-init race" was impossible (sync no-await initialize() is atomic on a single event loop — a lock would be dead defense); A4 was already-fixed in a prior session (audit read the pre-flight, missed the runtime-path fix); A1-contract-gate was by-design (no versioned FE/BE contract exists, and coupling would reintroduce a prior regression). Blind-fixing all 8 would have added a useless lock + an over-engineered contract mechanism. The audit found the surface; the skeptic decided real-vs-non-bug. (2026-07-18, run_2d3417d9, source:manual)
- [pitfall] Python except-clause ORDER is a data-loss trap when the narrow exception is a SUBCLASS of the broad one: my DB-integrity probe wrote `except sqlite3.DatabaseError: return False` (corrupt→re-seed) BEFORE `except sqlite3.OperationalError: return True` (locked→keep). But OperationalError IS a subclass of DatabaseError, so `except DatabaseError` catches "database is locked" first → the OperationalError branch is DEAD CODE → a valid-but-momentarily-locked data.db gets classified corrupt → unlink()+re-seed → USER DATA DESTROYED. The fix's own recovery logic was inverted by ordering. Rule: when catching a subclass and its parent, the SUBCLASS clause MUST come first; and any "detect-corrupt→destroy-and-recover" path needs a test that holds a real lock and asserts the data survives. Adversarial-caught HIGH; I verified via `issubclass(sqlite3.OperationalError, sqlite3.DatabaseError)`. (2026-07-18, run_2d3417d9, source:manual)
- [pitfall] A "delete the corrupt DB and re-seed" recovery must also delete the -wal/-shm sidecars — a malformed DB from a crash-mid-write is EXACTLY when a hot -wal exists, and unlinking only the main file leaves a stale foreign WAL that the fresh seed copy replays into → re-corruption. Recover the whole DB unit (db + -wal + -shm), not just the .db file. (2026-07-18, run_2d3417d9, source:manual)
- [guideline] Single-axis completeness analysis is a false "all clear": I reviewed "will startup block?" only along the BACKEND-readiness axis (daemon/health/onboarding) and declared "防呆做得很好" — while a deterministic every-launch boot crash sat on the FRONTEND-render axis I never walked (a root-mounted component calling useLayout() outside its provider). When claiming a GLOBAL property ("won't block"), enumerate the AXES first (backend-ready, frontend-render/context-boundaries, deploy-state) and state which were actually checked — an unwalked axis is not a passing axis. Worse: I had the clue (I listed CredentialBanner as a root-mounted useLayout consumer when drawing the startup diagram) and didn't connect "root node + useLayout = provider not upstream = throws". (2026-07-18, run_7f4388dd, source:manual)
- [guideline] Structural class-defense &gt; instance fix, and the loud/silent axis is load-bearing: to stop a recurring compile-time-invisible crash class, a Gate-0 skeptic flipped my design from "wrap every root component in ErrorBoundary fallback=null" (silent — MASKS the next violation, and fatal on the startup overlay: swallowing its crash strands isBackendReady=false = permanent boot hang) to "static source-scan test (LOUD, catches the class at its source) as primary + isolate ONLY passive banners as defense-in-depth, never the functional startup gate". A guard that hides violations is worse than the crash it prevents. (2026-07-18, run_7f4388dd, source:manual)
- [guideline] A guard/regression test needs its OWN mutation + adversarial pass — my class-guard had 3 real bugs my first draft missed, each caught by forcing it: (1) fallback={null} didn't isolate (ErrorBoundary guarded on truthiness → fell through to full-screen; R28 forced-exec test caught it), (2) the guard only forbade 1 of 6 shell-only hooks (meta-review coverage check found useTerminal/useTreeData/useSessionMeta unguarded — same crash class), (3) the list-drift self-check truncated its scan at the first inner `</ErrorBoundary>` (indexOf vs lastIndexOf) so it silently missed half the root block. The tell: a guard test that passes on your first try is under-tested — mutate it (inject the exact bug it guards) and adversarially probe its coverage. (2026-07-18, run_7f4388dd, source:manual)
- [guideline] The Gate-0 skeptic is a two-way filter, not just a pass-gate — run it on EVERY reported "latent/bug" including my own prior notes. Same session: it killed a proposed fix (WRONG-FRAME: merging the two 300s startup windows would regress run_e3dbc009's anti-false-kill) AND confirmed a real one (AppRoutes blank-screen dead-end). My own TECH.md "latent" note was the input that most needed challenging. (2026-07-18, run_834e952c + run_fdf5129c, source:manual)
- [guideline] react-query error state = no-exit dead-end pattern: a useQuery with `retry:N` and no `isError` branch renders null forever after retries exhaust (status=undefined, isLoading=false). Fix at the pure decision fn: add `isError` param, check it BEFORE the `status===undefined` clause but AFTER `isLoading` (so loading wins during retries, cached-status falls through, no flash). - [guideline] **Dog-fooding the coverage mechanism on real data found two wiring gaps unit tests couldn't** — (1) `compute_subsystem_coverage` credits a subsystem as "covered" ONLY via `steps[].file_path` / `entry_ref` route files, NOT `business_rules[].anchor` — so internal (non-HTTP) subsystems must anchor a flow to a real RELATED route (e.g. services-jobs→`/api/jobs/run`) to be counted. <!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [pitfall] **A self-authored anti-hallucination gate had its OWN escape hatch (CLASS A, caught by Gate-2)** — I built `check_mermaid_node_anchoring` (ai_ready_helpers.py) specifically to close the mermaid-node hole, and its docstring literally asserted "repo_root is NOT an escape hatch" — but Gate-2 proved it WAS: `(_root / tok).is_file()` accepts an ABSOLUTE token `/tmp/x.py` (pathlib discards the left operand when rhs is absolute) and `../` traversal, so the gate meant to block hallucinated nodes would accept ANY real `.py`/`.ts` file anywhere on the machine. The felt-completeness of a self-authored security check is indistinguishable from actual completeness — the exact authorship trap, surfacing in the very gate designed to ENFORCE anti-hallucination. Fix: `resolve()` + `relative_to(repo_root)` containment (the same discipline `_safe_file_read` ALREADY implements in the same file — I re-derived instead of reusing it). Mutation-verified (revert containment → 2 escape tests RED). **Durable tell: a path-based disk check with `is_file()` alone is NEVER containment — always `resolve()`+`relative_to()`, and grep the same file for an existing safe-path helper to reuse before writing a new one.** (2026-07-16, run_3026ef31)

- [pitfall] **Referential-integrity ("does the ref resolve?") is necessary but NOT sufficient — dog-food found a semantic-mismatch bug the gate structurally could not** — `flow:ws-file-write` declared `entry_ref` = the POST `create_file` route, but its mermaid AND all its steps described the PUT `put_workspace_file` path (step line-ranges land inside the PUT handler). `check_domain_referential_integrity` PASSED because the POST route id is a *valid* id — the gate only checks `entry_ref` resolves to *some* route, never that the entry_ref's method/path/file AGREES with the flow's own mermaid/steps. A human reading the spec would be pointed at the wrong entry point. Fixed by hand (correct PUT route id + spec regenerated). **Lesson: a "does X resolve?" gate never catches "does X resolve to the RIGHT thing?" — candidate future gate: assert a flow's `entry_ref` file_path is consistent with its steps' `file_path`.** Dog-fooding on real SwarmAI data surfaced this; fixtures never would. (2026-07-16, run_3026ef31)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- A "fix" for a timeout false-fatal can RE-INTRODUCE the same false-fatal one layer down — and only the Gate-2 meta-review caught it. The new-user first-launch false-fatal (fixed 60s startup cap killing a slow-but-alive daemon) was correctly re-based on launchd process-liveness (`launchctl pid present` = keep waiting), but the fail-fast leg (N consecutive no-pid checks → give up) would kill a daemon still inside the launchd **bootstrap→spawn window** — where the service is registered but `pid=` isn't populated yet on a disk-pressured 500MB cold start. That is the EXACT false-fatal being fixed, just at 16s instead of 60s. Fix: gate fail-fast on `ever_alive` — a never-yet-seen-alive process waits to the absolute ceiling (O030 backstop); only was-alive-then-gone (real crash/respawn-loop) fails fast. Lesson: when replacing a guillotine timeout with an adaptive one, enumerate EVERY "give up" leg and ask "does this fire during the exact slow-but-healthy scenario I'm fixing?" — the fail-fast optimization is where the regression hides. (2026-07-15, run_e3dbc009)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- The diagnose-before-build gate (THINK risk-probe) paid for the whole run by FALSIFYING my own EVALUATE root-cause sub-claim BEFORE any code. I framed the bug as "the gate discards the backend's `{status:initializing}` HTTP signal" — but `grep` of ~15k daemon-log lines showed `initializing` is served 0 times: uvicorn only binds the port AFTER lifespan startup completes (`_startup_complete=True` at main.py:1049, before the yield), so during the slow window the client gets connection-refused, never `initializing`. An HTTP-status-based fix would have been a NO-OP. The real alive/dead signal is the OS process (launchctl pid), not HTTP. Lesson: for any "the code ignores signal X" bug, VERIFY signal X is actually emitted at runtime (grep real logs) before building the consumer for it — a runtime-unreachable branch makes the fix theater. (2026-07-15, run_e3dbc009)

- SECTION_SCAFFOLD as single-source-of-truth killed a 4-copy drift class: the six-section structure lived in the scaffold dict, the pinning test, SKILL.md's §3.6 table, AND the spec — they drift independently (the test literally still pinned 5 READMEs + the old name). Fix (Gate-1 skeptic's): make the test DERIVE EXPECTED from SECTION_SCAFFOLD.keys()+SECTION_DIRS, so the structure can only be defined once. A structure that appears in N places should be defined in 1 and referenced N-1 times. (2026-07-12, run_693df058)
- A prune/migration that unlinks by PATH is a data-loss bug waiting to fire; content-gate it. Gate-2 reproduced live: `_prune_legacy_scaffold` deleted a human-authored `gates/README.md` because it matched a legacy PATH — no content check. Fix: unlink only if the file still carries the shipped-stub markers (a human edit diverges → kept). Same class: a `.gitkeep`-only dir must count as empty (else migrate leaves a half-state that violates the very invariant it enforces). Rule: destructive migration deletes by CONTENT-SIGNATURE, never by path alone; and "empty enough to remove" must exclude dotfile markers. (2026-07-12, run_693df058)
- DDD-native skill decoupling proved shippable in one goal run by keeping the MOAT and dropping the MACHINE ROOM: s_ddd-pipeline/s_ddd-pollinate rewrite s_autonomous-pipeline/s_pollinate to file-based `.artifacts/` state (no data.db/artifact_cli) while retaining Gate-2 adversarial-before-commit + the 养成 ladder. The test asserts BOTH halves — decoupled (uses run.json, states "no data.db") AND moat-intact (adversarial + ladder + BLOCKING) — so a future "port" that keeps the coupling OR drops the moat goes RED. Portability is "carries the brain + method, never the machine room." (2026-07-12, run_693df058)
- SwarmWS-native DDD ≠ AIM-export form: the recurring over-build (agents/, agent-sops/, per-section READMEs) came from conflating the §555 EXPORT layout with the §3.6 CANONICAL structure. The native skeleton is lean (① manifests + ⑥ marker + ③④ empty section dirs via .gitkeep + ONE unified AGENTS.md); the AIM-specific members generate at export. When a spec has both a "canonical" and a "physical realization" section, provision the canonical one — the physical members are an export concern. (2026-07-12, run_693df058)

- [guideline] **A false-positive terminal predicate is symmetric-dangerous to the bug it fixes** — Debugging-Rule + test suite caught an over-broad predicate BEFORE ship: my first is_terminal_run treated all-listed-stages-completed as terminal, which is exactly what a mid-pipeline PAUSE looks like (stages-done-so-far all completed + next_stage remaining). <!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [pitfall] **Authorship-trap recurred through a self-authored test — Gate-2 caught my INCOMPLETE fix (run_9290f4ff, full)** — Built `scripts/sync_discussions.py` (number-keyed docs/discussions mirror sync). During BUILD I hit a header-duplication bug on live data, "fixed" `extract_editorial_header` with an xref-regex guard, wrote a test (`> **In one line**` body, NO `#N`), watched it pass. **The fix was incomplete AND my test proved nothing:** the regex's `#\d+\b` alternative still matched a body-leading `> Related: #99` blockquote → duplicated on every UPDATE. All 22 self-authored tests were green; the fresh-context Gate-2 sub-agent found it in the "editorial header" attack vector because IT used a `#N` body (the case my test omitted — the exact blind spot of the author). Kernel: **"I wrote a test for it" ≡ "the behavior is guarded" is as false as "I wrote it" ≡ "it works" — a self-authored test covers what I ASSUMED, not what I missed.** The real fix was positional (header is glued to the marker; first blank line ends it), not a content regex — a content regex on `#N` can't distinguish an editorial cross-ref from a body cross-ref. **What held:** the mandatory Gate-2 spawn + then mutation-verifying the new regression test RED-on-revert (reintroduce the buggy loop → `test_body_leading_xref_blockquote_not_duplicated_on_update` goes RED). The tell to reuse: after fixing a parsing bug, ask "does my test include the case that looks IDENTICAL to the thing I'm trying to exclude?" — if not, it's theater. (2026-07-10, run_9290f4ff, source:pipeline)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:pipeline -->
- [decision] **Live-probe-before-scope saved a 70-file blast radius (run_9290f4ff, full)** — PLAN assumed the docs/discussions mirror was mostly frontmatter-style, so "edit-aware sync" would be low-blast. The BUILD `--check` against the REAL tree falsified that: 35/72 files are legacy no-frontmatter format + bodies are editorialized (not verbatim live copies), so a naive "regenerate from live" flagged 70/72 as drift. Running `--check` on real data at BUILD (not trusting the plan's assumption) exposed it before any `--write` → narrowed UPDATE to "live edited-date > mirror `updated:` AND has frontmatter", added LEGACY_SKIP (never auto-reformat), bounding blast radius to the actual drift class (missing files + stale index + genuine edits). Idempotency pivot also from a live probe: `updated:` MUST derive from stable `lastEditedAt||createdAt`, never `today()`, or every run drifts. LESSON: for a sync/migration tool, run the read-only mode against production data DURING build — the plan's assumption about the corpus shape is a hypothesis, and the corpus is the cheapest place to falsify it. (2026-07-10, run_9290f4ff, source:pipeline)

Real miss mode is short-CJK-low-signal-query, NOT synonymy — if ever revisited, the lever is CJK keyword handling in `_extract_query_keywords`, never stemming or vectors. **META: this is the 2nd headroom-derived idea this session that evidence killed (1st: comment-drift). - [decision] **Comment-drift detector hook — SHELVED v1 at Gate 1, correct outcome (run_796f8227, bugfix)** — Two gates killed a net-negative feature with ZERO code shipped: EVALUATE M3 skeptic reframed adjacency→content-linkage; Gate 1 plan-skeptic then proved even content-linkage is net-negative. **The hard constraint for any future attempt:** the feature's value IS precision (an annoying reminder is worse than none), but deterministic token-overlap ("a surviving comment shares a token with changed code") false-positives on the MODAL case — any comment naming a common identifier (config/data/result/client) fires on nearly every same-name edit → ~8 real historical cases vs hundreds of spurious reminders = inverted SNR = F004 fatigue. Hand-rolled triple-quote/comment parsing (no AST) is also defeated by ordinary Python (f/r-strings, `"""` in string literals, single-line docstrings, inline comments, reindent). **The ONLY low-FP design is AST symbol-level** (fire only when a comment references a symbol whose definition/signature changed) — a larger scope that must be re-EVALUATEd on its own, never smuggled into a bugfix. LESSON (C042/C044 family): the idea came from a headroom research report — chasing a capability because a reference repo has it, not because I feel the pain, is the "修表面 / build-because-they-have-it" kernel; quantification + Gate 1 both said stop, and stopping was the win. (2026-07-10, run_796f8227, source:manual)

The right fix was backend-primary: classify `pause_kind` via the canonical `_CRASH_ZOMBIE_REASON`/`is_terminal_run` (single source of truth, already reused by `proactive_intelligence.py:857`) and have the frontend CONSUME the verdict, never re-derive it. **Reusable: when a symptom shows in a UI widget, the fix layer is often the backend that feeds it — a frontend string-match on data it only partially has is a weaker re-invention of a discriminator the backend already owns. Ask "who else consumes this?" before choosing the layer.** Also validated the fail-direction: an attention queue must fail-SHOW on uncertainty (null/absent classification → show), never fail-hide. Gate-0 (skeptic) correctly REFUTED my "must use a retire-scoped matcher" premise by proving Open Threads is section-evergreen-guarded — so a global widen is "safe for decay." Gate-1 verified that against `assess_decay`+`reclaim`. BOTH gates checked the SAME single path (decay) and both passed. Gate-2 (fresh adversary) found the premise was only 1/3 true: `_ENTRY_RE` also feeds `inject_entry_metadata` (would STAMP `<!-- ref -->` onto 8 curated OT bullets — verified live on real MEMORY.md, delta +8) and the `ddd_orchestrator` IMPROVEMENT path (reclaim strips with NO evergreen set). The section-guard covers decay ONLY; inject+reclaim were unguarded. **The reusable lesson: when a change touches a SHARED primitive (a regex, a constant, a parser used at N sites), "safe" must be proven at EVERY consumer, not the one the design happened to examine — grep ALL callers and check each, because the gate that blesses the first path gives false confidence about the rest.** The convergence fix vindicated the retire-SCOPED matcher Gate-1 had called "redundant": make prose-matching OPT-IN so autonomous write-paths are safe BY CONSTRUCTION (can't see prose), not by remembering to pass evergreen everywhere. Safe-by-construction > guard-you-must-remember. (2026-07-03, run_748f14a7)

- Stale test-mock drift is a silent eval-decay class: a test that patch()es a symbol the code NO LONGER calls does not fail loud — the real unmocked call runs, gets caught by a broad except, and returns status=error, so the test is ALWAYS-RED but looks like a normal failure. Tell: an assert error==passed on an LLM-judge test = the mock target drifted off the code path. Fix = repoint to the symbol the code actually imports (call-time from X import Y → patch the SOURCE module attribute). (2026-07-02, run_2453b416, auto-cultivated)

- prewarm sessions ran the full ~11-hook post-session chain (~6.5s) for nothing — 24h prod logs: 32 prewarm spawned / 0 adopted / 28 orphan-reaped, each paying 6.5s of auto-commit/evolution/context-health. Root cause was a MISSING GUARD at the single chokepoint (LifecycleManager.enqueue_hooks): every reap/idle/TTL/eviction path funnels through it, none checked the prewarm prefix. Fix = 1 guard on session_id.startswith(PREWARM_SESSION_PREFIX) + early return; AC3 (post-adoption resume) came FREE because adopt_prewarmed_unit re-keys unit.session_id to a real id and _build_hook_context reads it live. Gate-1 skeptic's leak-check was the load-bearing step: confirmed all 11 hooks touch only workspace/knowledge (never subprocess/session-state), so skipping is safe — without that check, skipping hooks could have leaked resources. Chose id-prefix guard over a new is_prewarm flag (DDD: new mutable state on session_unit/router god-files = top regression source). (2026-06-30, run_f10075d3)
- OT01 diagnosis sharpened: "is the pipeline stuck?" was a FRONTEND render freeze, not a backend hang. The discriminator that settled it in seconds: `ps` (the bg pytest was already gone) + run-get (stages all completed) — observe-the-live-system beat guessing. When a long pytest gets auto-backgrounded, the frontend spinner can desync from backend streaming = OT01 class; refresh the tab, don't re-run. (2026-06-30, run_f10075d3)

- A score-drift GATE must compare against a DIFFERENT-code_digest baseline, NOT the raw previous report. Comparing latest vs a same-code RE-RUN measures LLM-judge noise, not regression — proven on real data: 100.0→91.7 on identical digest 526db7a385 (an 8.3pt "drop" that is pure noise). The gate walks reports newest-first by MTIME (NOT filename — _load_history's filename sort disagrees with the gate's mtime sort when filename formats coexist) and picks the first different-digest report as baseline; fail-OPEN when no different-code baseline exists. "Score changed" ≠ "my change regressed score." (2026-06-30, run_95d9acbc)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [decision] **Stop → COLD subprocess recycle is INTENTIONAL — do NOT "keep it warm" (KDD, verified 2026-06-30)** — The perceived regression "Stop 后再 resume 很慢" (~14-16s cold-spawn on the next message) is BOUNDED LATENCY, not breakage/data-loss, and it must be left alone. Mechanism is PIT01 (recorded): after ANY soft interrupt the Claude CLI subprocess is in corrupt turn-state; reusing it returns an instant empty `error_during_execution` (zombie) the detector kills + respawns anyway — so eager recycle (`interrupt_recycle` session_unit ~2789 / `flush_recycle` ~2599 → `_crash_to_cold_async` → COLD) just FRONT-LOADS a kill that would happen lazily otherwise. **The 15s is paid EITHER WAY**: skipping the recycle hits the zombie path next message = same ~15s PLUS a failed round-trip + 5s retry backoff, AND resurrects the exact bug PIT01 fixed. "Conditionally keep warm" fights an SDK behavior and is high-regression-risk in the session_unit/session_router god-files (source of nearly every past lifecycle regression). **IF the 15s ever must be addressed:** (1) ZERO-RISK — a frontend "正在恢复会话…" indicator during cold-spawn (display-only, never touches lifecycle); (2) REAL fix (higher risk, only if justified) — reuse the EXISTING prewarm machinery (`prewarm_channel_session` + `adopt_prewarmed_unit`, session_router ~887/963, currently Slack-only) to background-spawn a `--resume` replacement during user think-time, needing a `--resume` prewarm variant + compute_max_tabs budget gate + tab-alive gate + a test that ENTERS each path. Do NOT conditionally-keep-warm without first PROVING the zombie isn't universal. Verified this session: spec `.kiro/specs/subprocess-recycle-on-interrupt/requirements.md` exists; all five symbols confirmed present at the cited locations. (Source: Kiro parallel-session KDD, verified against live code; mechanism = PIT01/PIT48, this entry adds the decision boundary.) (2026-06-30, source:manual)

- A fail-closed GUARD on a primitive can make the COMMON path worse than the bug it fixes — if the guard fires on the normal usage pattern. run_3caef1d3: `_find_active_run` picked the project-wide newest active run with no owner scoping → `publish --stage` without `--run-id` contaminated a sibling run. I added a fail-closed guard (2+ active + no --run-id → exit 3). Gate-2 found: ALL 8 stage docs publish WITHOUT --run-id, so in a multi-run workspace (the bug's own precondition — "14 active runs") EVERY publish would now hit the guard and HALT the pipeline. The guard alone converted silent contamination into a loud stall. The fix needed BOTH halves: the guard (backstop) AND threading --run-id through the 8 stage docs (remove the trigger). Lesson: when you guard a primitive against a bad input, grep how callers ACTUALLY invoke it — if the guard fires on the documented/common call shape, you've shipped a worse regression. (2026-06-29, run_3caef1d3)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none | source:pipeline -->
- A fail-closed error must be emitted on the SAME stream the consumer's error-handler reads. run_3caef1d3: my ambiguity error went to STDOUT, but the documented orchestrator guard is `OUT=$(publish … 2>/tmp/pub.err) || { cat /tmp/pub.err; }` — it reads STDERR on failure and feeds STDOUT to `json.load(...)['artifact_id']`. So the actionable error was invisible AND would crash the guard's json.load. Gate-2 caught it by reading the consumer contract, not just the producer. Fix: match the sibling validation_failed path (stderr). Tell: a new error path must be routed to wherever the EXISTING error paths go — grep how failures are already surfaced before inventing a new channel. (2026-06-29, run_3caef1d3)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- A regression-guard test that builds its own throwaway probe instead of asserting on the REAL fixture/SUT is vacuous test-theater (CLASS A) — Gate-2 caught a guard that stayed green when the fixture was reverted to the bug; fix: monkeypatch the real app lifespan + request.getfixturevalue the real fixture, mutation-proven RED on revert. (2026-06-29, run_b9ecb07a, auto-cultivated)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none | source:pipeline -->
- A regression-guard test that builds its OWN throwaway probe instead of asserting on the REAL fixture/SUT is VACUOUS test-theater — it proves a general framework fact, not that THIS code is fixed. run_b9ecb07a: a guard for "the `client` fixture must not run lifespan" built a `probe_app` with a tripwire lifespan and a bare `TestClient(probe_app)` — it stayed GREEN when the real fixture was reverted to the buggy `with`-form (mutation-tested), so it guarded nothing. The "mutation-proven" claim in the commit was false (CLASS A authorship trap, test layer). Gate-2 adversarial caught it. Fix: assert against the REAL thing — `monkeypatch.setattr(main.app.router, "lifespan_context", tripwire)` + invoke the actual fixture via `request.getfixturevalue("client")`; now mutation-proven RED on revert. Tell: if your guard imports/constructs a stand-in of the SUT instead of the SUT, it's theater. (2026-06-29, run_b9ecb07a)

- `with TestClient(app)` runs the app's FULL lifespan (startup+shutdown); a bare `TestClient(app)` (no context manager) does NOT — Starlette only enters lifespan on `__enter__` (verified Starlette 0.50.0). A unit test that only hits a light endpoint (/health) and forces `_startup_complete=True` does NOT need lifespan; using the `with`-form makes every such test pay the real DB-init+migrations+workspace-ensure cost (main.py:693+). run_b9ecb07a: two fixtures (test_daemon_sidecar_fixes + _robustness) did this → an R7 pytest exceeded the 120s foreground timeout → Bash auto-backgrounded → empty foreground → UI tool-spinner stranded at "11m34s". Same-class scan (Gate-2) found the sibling file; fixing only one leaves the incident live. (2026-06-29, run_b9ecb07a)

- [guideline] **Gate-2 adversarial earned its keep on a trivial-profile guard: caught a HIGH false-kill rooted in an unverified runtime-state assumption (run_07fd1d8f, bash_syntax_guard).** I built a PreToolUse Bash guard that parse-checks each command with `<shell> -n` before it runs (DENY the syntactically-incomplete/hang set, fail-open otherwise) — and hardcoded `/bin/bash`, never verifying the Bash tool actually runs `zsh`. The whole EVALUATE understanding rested on that unchecked assumption (R16b / CLASS-B). The fresh-context adversarial sub-agent found it: `/bin/bash -n` rejects valid zsh syntax (`foo() { echo hi }`) → the guard would false-kill legitimate commands (the cardinal sin for a hang-prevention guard). Two transferable lessons: (1) **A guard that validates commands must validate with the SAME interpreter that executes them** — verify the exec shell, never assume. (2) **R16b applies to sub-agent claims too**: the adversary's own example (`for i in 1 2 3 { }` "is zsh-valid") was WRONG — I ran `printf|zsh -n` (exit 1, invalid) before trusting it and used the actually-valid `for i (1 2 3) { }`. Don't trust a sub-agent's runtime-behavior claim any more than your own; verify both. Honest residual: `-n` only catches PARSE-incomplete hangs; a syntactically-valid command that blocks on stdin at runtime (bare `cat`/`read`/`grep`) parses fine and is approved — a different hang class, not claimed covered. (2026-06-29, run_07fd1d8f, manual)

- [guideline] **Session-briefing assembly cut 1150ms→~280ms warm (-75%) by mtime-pre-filtering an unbounded scan (run_885eb466).** Root cause: `_get_paused_pipeline_highlights` (proactive_intelligence.py:752) `json.loads`'d ALL 343 run.json (SwarmAI alone 327, 1.5MB) every assembly, then filtered by `updated_at` — but the function's semantics only ever wanted runs <24h old. Fix: a cheap `st_mtime` gate before the read, skipping files untouched >48h (2× max_age buffer). Three transferable lessons: (1) **Unbounded scan feeding bounded output (PIT90 sibling)** — reading 343 files to pick `max_items=3` directives; push the existing window-filter DOWN to a cheap stat layer instead of read-then-filter. (2) **Profile perf in warm AND cold** — the first profiling pass blamed `_get_health_highlights` (1183ms) + DDD (297ms), but those were one-time import tax (jobs/eval/ddd heavy modules); warm they're <40ms. Only `_get_paused_pipeline_highlights` was a true resident cost. Not splitting warm/cold = fixing the wrong target. (3) **A prior "optimized" claim can miss the real bottleneck** — commit 56209561 claimed briefing→4ms but it stayed 1.3s; it had cut a different section, the real hot path was never touched. The long-suspected Recurrence Radar (reads 407K IMPROVEMENT.md) was measured at 6ms — innocent. (2026-06-29, run_885eb466, manual)

- [guideline] **Adding a streaming-render timer safely = 3 traps, all solved by copying useStreamingActivity's discipline (run_02e658d0).** A per-second timer in the chat render path is near the OT01 reconcile / cross-tab zones. The 3 traps: (1) anchor on the per-invocation id (toolUseId), NOT mount-time — React reuses keyed-list instances, a mount anchor shows the prior tool's time; (2) gate the setInterval on the active/pending flag — tabs are keep-mounted, an ungated interval leaves N idle timers on every completed card across every tab (the F4 hazard); (3) always clear on unmount AND on the gate flipping false. Keeping it client-side (useRef/useState only, no stored field, no MessageStore write, no SSE coupling, no module-level state) means it scopes to its TabView instance by construction and never touches the reconcile/isolation fragile zones. (2026-06-28, run_02e658d0, manual)

The user-named heavy sections were FALSIFIED by measurement: Recurrence Radar reading the 390K IMPROVEMENT.md = 5ms; _get_paused_pipeline_highlights = the PIT01-protected auto-resume engine. The REAL cost was a bare `EvalService().growth_report(7)` buried 3 calls deep in `_get_health_highlights`: it re-parsed 84 EvalHistory files every briefing (516ms) + spawned a git-log subprocess that contended on the repo lock under N-tab concurrency (the 8.4s spike). - **Briefing cognition-admission: cut 8 feed sections from the system-prompt briefing, gated by 3 adversarial passes (run_a16d61ad).** The session briefing is COGNITION real-estate (it goes straight into the system prompt, competing with 11 context files for attention — F004), not a dashboard. Drove "什么进 briefing" by a KEEP/CUT/RECALL admission test, finalized the whole READ line in one goal run (5 commits, 8/8 DoD, 210 tests green, eval 100%). Four reusable lessons: (1) **Gate-0 (understanding) is worth a full skeptic pass even when you "know" the system** — it REFRAMED work-stream B: `_get_paused_pipeline_highlights` LOOKS like a display read but is the auto-resume ENGINE (mutates resume_attempts under fcntl lock, orphan→paused, abandoned); my planned "cap to newest 10 by mtime" would have silently DROPPED a paused run with a pending resume directive. A "cheap perf cap" on a function with side effects is a data-loss bug. KILLED. (2) **Gate-1 (plan-attack) caught an assumed-wire-that-isn't-there**: work-stream C (wire CodeIntel into the live recall leg) assumed `_recall_for_query`/`unit` carry a bound project — they don't (no session→project binding in the recall path). Threading it through would be a signature ripple on the hottest path for a trigger signal that isn't even available (the C042 "build a mechanism" trap). SPLIT: the CUT half (remove codebase-map from briefing) fully delivers the PUSH→PULL intent; the WIRE half deferred to a run that first builds the binding infra. (3) **Live-data falsification before cutting beats intuition**: DDD-trust READ as 1273 `trust:high` : 2 `trust:low` (both empty-doc placeholders) → zero discrimination → the section was occupying cognition without informing. Measured, not guessed. (4) **Cross-path adversarial on the TOTAL changeset caught the field-init question that per-commit review couldn't**: a parallel layer added `_recall_keyword_misses` init in `SessionUnit.__init__` + reset in `_cleanup_internal` — the final adversary verified ALL unit-construction paths (create/resume/COLD-respawn) run __init__ and the getattr-default backstops it, so the recall#5 miss-cap can't AttributeError. Per-commit review sees one file; the cross-path pass sees the seam. (2026-06-28, run_a16d61ad, manual)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- **goal pipeline drove a 2-Run subsystem removal to 10/10 DoD in one run (run_e9b8507e, pure-filesystem recall impl).** "Run1+Run2 一起做完别停" → goal profile, 10 DoD, BUILD+TEST looped to green. Two clean commits (aaed814a read-line + 6540970e vector-removal), eval 56/56 100%, 2 adversarial passes both SHIP. Three reusable lessons: (1) **Removing a subsystem (vector) safely = reader AND writer stopped in the SAME run + golden cases retired in the same run** — stopping readers but leaving the embed writer would burn Bedrock writing vectors nobody reads (design §5.5 caught this; the adversary verified all 3 writers null-guard embed_fn=None); leaving the vector golden cases would leave the eval gate RED. (2) **A by-function script deletion swallows module-level constants that sit BETWEEN the deleted functions** — my `_onehot`/`_build_db` deletion took out `_ENTRY_MARKER` (a const between two probes) and over-removed a still-used `patch` import; the survivor-path run (`codeintel_live`/`resume_fill`/`entry_recall` positive+negative) caught both as NameError before commit. Always run the SURVIVING paths after a bulk delete, not just "it imports". (3) **When a spec changes (truncate→never-truncate, vector→keyword), the probes/tests that encoded the OLD spec must be rewritten to the NEW contract IN THE SAME run, with teeth re-verified** — `assembly_floor` INV2/INV3 (truncation order) were deleted and replaced with INV1 byte-intact + INV2 no-marker; the negative path still bites (broken-enforcer → RED). A retired-but-not-rewritten probe is either a false-green or a permanent RED. (2026-06-28, run_e9b8507e, manual)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- **Autonomous-pipeline 近期(06-24~06-28)的提速来自"少跑偏=少跑",不是单轮变快 — 已 git 核验。** 近4天 34 个 pipeline commit,grep `perf/latency/timeout/cache/token` = **0 命中**:pipeline 自身没有任何降低单轮 LLM 延迟的改动。真正压低"一个 coding task 总墙钟"的是砍掉白做的 turn,机制有四条且都核验存在:(1) **Understanding Gate + 自苏格拉底歧义扫描** (`2479fed2`+`5389c2ca`,EVALUATE→THINK 边界) → 不再 BUILD 到一半发现方向错回炉(回炉一次=几十 turn 蒸发);(2) **REPRO gate + diagnostic-challenge** (`58bb0287`) → bug 任务先复现再动手,少一轮改错地方;(3) **profile-selection 修复 goal 过度选中** (`53dd788c`,改 INSTRUCTIONS.md+evaluate.md) → 本该 trivial/bugfix 的任务不再被塞进最重的 goal 档跑多个 cycle,**直接砍总耗时**;(4) **in-band L2 escalation via AskUserQuestion** (`8952cbe7`) → 卡住时当场问,不再 auto-resume 三次后干等(补 run_6c482b10 的 DELIVERY gap)。**最大混淆因素(必须扣除):06-26 `cabf2cc0` 单分支合并**(确认 2 parents)— 在那之前仓库分裂成两条未合并并行分支,每次 build 只带一半修复、fix 在两次部署间 flip-flop;合并后 build 第一次带齐 ~200+ commits 全部累积修复。所以"突然顺了"里有相当一部分是"终于跑在统一带齐修复的 build 上",不是某个 perf commit。**认知要点:在 coding pipeline 语境里,"顺"(少跑偏)和"快"(总墙钟)不是两件事 — 耗时≈stage数×每stage turn数,砍轮次就是真提速,只是省在轮次数量而非单轮速度。把 pipeline 改动一律降级成"只让流程顺"会低估真实提速。** 边界:本结论从 commit 内容核验,未取 run 的实测 stage 耗时;要坐实须比两个同类 run 的 `REPORT.md` 的 stage总数/总turn/abandon次数(不是单 stage 秒数)。(2026-06-28, manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- **3 cascading gates each caught a DIFFERENT defect-class on a docs-only design (run_19fe8649, Context/Recall pure-filesystem 设计).** This was the strongest single-run evidence yet for "model proposes, OS disposes." In ONE session I produced THREE confidently-wrong root-cause framings of a cold-start slowness — (a) "Titan embed 9.6s aborts system-prompt assembly" (REFUTED: that was a *theoretical timeout ceiling* I computed, never observed; real warm embed = 0.4s), (b) "load_all = 11s" (REFUTED: isolated load_all = 3s), (c) the whole "vector is the bottleneck" causal story — all falsified only when I finally ran empirical probes (O008) instead of inferring from constants (R16b violated 3×). The design that emerged was then gated: **Gate-0** (framing skeptic) caught that recall has TWO legs (injection-leg vector is dead code; runtime-leg vector is live) — my framing had conflated them; **Gate-1** (design skeptic) BLOCKED with 3 code-verified defects (DDD-project-binding AC was architecturally impossible — production `_recall_for_query` never calls `recall_all`; deletion list missed the 3rd Titan reader `context_recall.recall_context` default `allow_embed=True`; §7 mis-classified `GS_RCHAIN_RECALL_BUDGET` as vector-independent when its probe asserts `embed_calls>0`); **Gate-2** (adversarial) caught a HIGH that would break Run 1 (deleting the `memory_embeddings` *parameter* KeyErrors `test_memory_wiring.py:32`, contradicting the "zero eval risk" claim) + 2 MED (3 vector-bound cases share fate; Run-2 "all green" DoD is unachievable for vector-premise cases → needs explicit retire-vs-rewrite rule). The durable lesson: **felt-certainty in a root-cause narrative is indistinguishable from a correct one — the empirical probe and the adversarial gate are the ONLY things that separated them. My model-layer error rate did not drop (3 wrong framings in one session); CONTAINMENT held (every design defect caught pre-ship).** Do not read a clean gated delivery as "I diagnosed well" — I diagnosed badly and the gates saved it. (2026-06-28, run_19fe8649, manual)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- **Grill-the-spec caught a self-contradicting requirement before it became a design (run_19fe8649).** XG proposed "recall 范围:Knowledge 只看最近1周 files." Grilling it against `find -mtime -7` data exposed the fatal contradiction: recall's PURPOSE is to surface what's NOT in the full-load context (= archived/old long-term memory), but a 1-week mtime window cuts EXACTLY that — Archives are old by definition, so the window would let recall see only what it least needs (new files already in DailyActivity). The right boundary for "怕 grep 太散" is BM25 top-N relevance (full scan, narrow return), NOT a time window. XG withdrew the constraint. Lesson: when a scoping rule is proposed, test it against the SUBSYSTEM'S PURPOSE with real data — a perf-guard (time window) aimed at the wrong axis can silently defeat the feature's whole reason to exist. (2026-06-28, run_19fe8649, manual)

- **Gate placement is a design decision, not an afterthought: a quality gate belongs at the action's FREQUENCY-vs-COST inflection point (run_aba84c36, 2026-06-26).** The eval gate was first wired on `build` — but `build` is a high-frequency dev action (run many times a session), so the gate stalled iteration; `release` (rare, ships to others) is where "must pass Eval first" actually belongs. XG's correction "build 不能拦,只有发版才能拦" is the general rule: gate the rare-but-irreversible boundary, never the frequent-and-cheap one. Moving it = pure win (faster dev loop + same ship safety). Tell: if a gate fires on something you do dozens of times a day, it's on the wrong layer.

- [pitfall] **FTS5 external-content desync: the 'delete' command MUST bind OLD stored values, not the new ones (run_1d198980, 2026-06-26)** — Library `knowledge_fts` "database disk image is malformed" had a confident wrong diagnosis ("content table intact, just rebuild it"). The M3 skeptic (Gate 0) overturned it: the real root cause was `knowledge_store.py:189` — `upsert_chunk`'s update branch bound the **NEW** content/heading/source_file to the FTS5 `'delete'` command. External-content FTS5 needs the **OLD** stored values to correctly reverse the posting lists; binding new values decrements the wrong terms → progressive index corruption on every content update. Precedent was right there: `messages_fts` (sqlite.py:1977) uses `old.content`, and the `remove_*` methods read old values first. Rebuild alone is a band-aid — the buggy write re-corrupts it. Mutation-proven: reverting :189 reproduces the EXACT production error on the 2nd update. Two further wins this run: (1) **self-found a real bug in my own health probe by observation, not assumption** — `_fts_is_healthy` queried a no-match term ("healthprobe") that never traverses the corrupted posting lists, so it returned True on a corrupt index; a health check that misses the corruption it guards is worse than none (fixed to query an existing term + added a mutation-proven probe-detects-corruption test). (2) An **injected-corruption test was HARDER than the live corruption** and exposed that `'rebuild'` itself fails on a deeply-desynced index → forced the `drop+recreate` nuclear fallback (data-safe: content lives in `knowledge_chunks`, 27587 rows). Repair lives in `context_health_hook`, NOT the read path — recall stays read-only. Scope discipline: TranscriptStore.upsert_chunk has the same latent class (INSERT OR REPLACE without preceding 'delete') — flagged, not fixed (separate table, separate run). (run_1d198980)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->
- [decision] **Eval system-level decouple = data-boundary move, not code-rewrite (run_69b1c644+run_45cc58fa, 2026-06-26)** — Moving Eval out of `Projects/SwarmAI/` to top-level `SwarmWS/Eval/` decoupled it from DDD with surgical edits (3 path-builders + marker keyed on `Eval/golden_set.yaml`) instead of a rewrite. EVALUATE skeptic reading the code found the gate was already 80% decoupled via `programmatic_only`; the real work was the ship-boundary split. Migration was pure exposure-REDUCTION (all 161 were already public; moving 128 to gitignored private only reduces leak surface). `git mv` for tracked (public+EvalHistory), plain `mv` for gitignored private (git mv errors on untracked). Pattern: for "decouple X from Y", first probe how coupled they ACTUALLY are — the answer is often "less than the requirement assumes". (run_45cc58fa)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->
- [decision] **Git-bound eval gate: bind freshness to INPUTS not HEAD; 3-state not 2 (run_7a0ff10d, 2026-06-26)** — `ci_eval_gate.py` binds `code_digest` to the eval-relevant code+golden_set CONTENT, not git HEAD — so committing the eval report (which changes HEAD) doesn't self-invalidate the gate (the lock-file fixed-point trap). 3-state exit is what makes it shippable: exit1 (stale|red) BLOCKS, but exit2 (no gate-readable report) SOFT-warns + proceeds — without that a fresh clone / bootstrap with no report would be permanently un-shippable. Gate-2 caught the `set -e` interaction: `_x=$(cmd)` aborts the whole script on non-zero substitution BEFORE `$?` is read — wrap gate calls in `set +e/set -e`. **NOTE (run_aba84c36, 2026-06-26): the gate was later moved off `build` to the RELEASE boundary** (XG: "build 不能拦,只有发版才能拦") — build is high-frequency dev; gating it stalled iteration. Now in shared `_eval_gate()` covering cmd_release + cmd_release_hive + s_swarm-release PREFLIGHT, with a non-TTY fail-closed path and a `_EVAL_GATE_PASSED` once-per-process guard. (run_7a0ff10d → run_aba84c36)

- [pitfall] **Security redaction: allowlist not denylist; LIST and DETAIL are separate leak surfaces (run_1f588e53, 2026-06-26)** — Exposing a public/private badge on the eval golden set needed private-case content hidden. A denylist (strip 6 named fields) felt complete and LEAKED TWICE (Gate-2): missed `expected_response_contains` (answer keys) via detail, and `source` (governance refs) via the separate list-projection endpoint. Fix: invert to allowlist (fail-closed — new fields dropped by default) AND audit every endpoint returning the object, not just the visible drawer. When you reach for a denylist on security-sensitive data, that's the signal to invert it. (run_1f588e53)

- Pure-logic-with-injected-deps test → MUST pair with one real-wiring test. (run_f646b175) My AC1 probe test injected a fake `rss_fetcher`, so it passed while the REAL `_fetch_rss` called a non-existent method (`get_system_memory`) reading non-existent fields → the RSS check was SILENTLY DEAD (fail-safe-passed forever). Adversarial Gate-2 found both CRITICALs. This is GUI01 (authorship blind spot) in its purest mechanical form: injecting all dependencies makes a test prove the LOGIC while leaving the WIRING (the adapter that constructs the real deps) completely uncovered. The fix pattern: for every pure-logic unit with injected I/O, add ≥1 test that exercises the real adapter against the live resource. (2026-06-26, verified)

- Reversible Context Recall (run_9de88af9): Gate-2 adversarial caught 2 CRITICAL data-leak bypasses of the AC4 privacy gate that my OWN AC4 tests missed — case-insensitivity (`memory.md` ≠ `MEMORY.md` but APFS reads the same file) and path-traversal (`../MEMORY.md`). My self-authored tests covered only the happy denial path (`recall("MEMORY.md", excluded={MEMORY.md})`), never the variants. This is GUI01 (authorship blind spot) recurring on a SECURITY gate: the most dangerous place to trust your own tests is exactly where you wrote both the guard and its test.
