- [guideline] **Researching two external references in parallel produced an executable design, not a prose summary — and they cross-validated the shape (2026-06-26, Understanding Gate design)** — Asked to evaluate the pipeline against `awslabs/aidlc-workflows` v2 and `aws-samples/sample-ai-plc`, the output that earned its keep was a DESIGN DOC + a tracked open item, NOT a "here's what they do" report. The decisive value: the two repos converged on the SAME structure from opposite ends — ai-plc gives the *implementation* (envision.md problem-only and solution-analysis.md are separate files with a `⛔ GATE` between; a dedicated `overconfidence-prevention.md` that inverts defaults from "ask only if needed" to "ask unless exceptionally clear"), and aidlc-v2 gives the *theory* (P3: same-source generate+judge converges to satisfying-the-letter, so an LLM-only-verified stage can't self-stop = the theoretical NAME for CLASS A; P6: staged decomposition beats single-shot). Convergence from two independent sources = high confidence the "diagnosis↔solution wall" shape is right, not a one-off idea. METHOD that generalizes: (1) when researching to improve our own system, the deliverable is a falsifiable design + open item, not restated facts (Research Quality Gate); (2) prefer TWO references that approach from different angles — agreement between them is the signal; (3) the design must address the SELF-DIAGNOSED gap (here Gap 1: dive-deep before build), with the failures it would have caught named explicitly (run_6adee7d5 `[DONE]` no-op). NOT a competitive-threat read — a mirror + theory-backing read (avoids COR03 self-underrating: we already shipped the artifact_id gate they're still drafting in issue #366). Design: `Knowledge/Designs/2026-06-26-understanding-gate-design.md`. (2026-06-26, source:manual)

EXCLUDED: `interrupt(autonomous=True)` = tool-hang watchdog ONLY (keeps STREAMING, lets the model reroute warm). **Production-verified 02:19**: user Stop → `flush_recycle` → recycled to COLD (resume id preserved) → next send spawned fresh pid → direct idle→streaming, zombie count 32 UNCHANGED (0 new). The disconnect path firing in the live test (not the interrupt path) retroactively proved Gate-1 finding-6 right: fixing only `interrupt()` would have left this exact case broken. - **OT01 spinner-hang root cause found by 3-SIGNAL LOG TRIAGE, not by reading code — and an external review corrected my mis-framed fix mid-flight.** (run_6adee7d5, 2026-06-26). The recurring "spinner spins after backend idle" (~33 fixes, all on the force-clear *backstop*) was finally root-caused by counting THREE mutually-exclusive frontend log signals: premature-disconnect (2), stall-timer 90s (3), and silent (~109) out of 114 idle force-clears. The 109 silent = `[DONE]` arrived + `onComplete` fired but `createCompleteHandler` early-returned on a STALE `streamGen` (bumped mid-stream by reconnect — the 65s reconnect budget widened the window) BEFORE `setIsStreaming(false)` → spinner pinned, rescued only by the 30s reconcile backstop. Fix: per-tab `latestCompleteGen` marks the live completer (immune to reconnect churn, superseded only by a new send). TWO external corrections shaped this: (1) Kiro flagged my ORIGINAL requirement ("make frontend treat [DONE] as authoritative") as a NO-OP — chat.ts:276 already does that; the real defect was the stale-gen gate, not [DONE] recognition. I had started building the wrong fix. (2) Gate-2 adversarial caught my fix introduced a FAIL-OPEN (`latestCompleteGen ?? capturedGen` = always-clear when unset) → hardened to fail-safe (unset = non-authoritative no-op). META-LESSON: for a bug that recurred 33× on the backstop, the discriminator was never in the code — it was in COUNTING the runtime signals to prove WHICH of the candidate mechanisms actually fired. Inference ("frontend ignores [DONE]") was wrong; signal-counting ("109 silent, not disconnect, not stall") was right. And both my framing AND my fix had a flaw that only fresh eyes (Kiro + adversarial) caught — confidence was anti-correlated with correctness, again.

- **Adversarial Gate-2 caught a HIGH that made the entire feature DEAD in production — a self-authored unit test could not, by construction.** Dumb-spawn watchdog (run_6c482b10): the discriminator `_last_event_time <= _streaming_start_time` was meant to detect "no SDK event since spawn." But `_transition()` set the two fields via TWO separate `time.time()` calls, so `_last_event_time` was always microseconds GREATER → the branch NEVER fired in prod → the watchdog the whole pipeline was built for did nothing. All 8 unit tests passed because the fixture set the two timestamps EQUAL — it encoded the author's assumption, not production's two-call reality. This is the CLASS-A authorship trap in its purest form: "I wrote the test, so it's covered" ≠ "the test reproduces production." THE FIX THAT GENERALIZES: for any predicate over runtime-set state, add ONE integration test that drives the REAL state-producer (here: call real `_transition` and assert the predicate holds) — not just unit tests with hand-set values. Proof the test works: reintroduce the bug → integration test goes RED (verified); the equal-timestamp unit fixtures stay GREEN (blind). A fixture that hand-sets the value you're testing is structurally incapable of catching a producer that sets it differently. (2026-06-26, run_6c482b10)

- [guideline] **Idempotency teeth must be at EVERY merge/record path, not just the entry point** — run_448a4f7f autonomous-recording: the SAME failure class (counter inflation from non-idempotent counting) fired at TWO layers. Gate-1 caught dedup-on-evidence-text (live correction_ref d6d44889 was parked 3× with 3 different evidence strings → text-dedup triple-counts; fix: dedup on stable correction_ref). Gate-2 then caught `_merge_drift` summing count across drifted members but keeping only members[0].recorded_refs → a dropped ref re-records and re-inflates. A counter is only as idempotent as its LEAST-guarded write path. When adding a dedup ledger, audit every path that mutates the count (record, merge, load) — fix one, the others still leak. (2026-06-25)

- _persist_golden_set cross-process TOCTOU close (run_0fac5a91, commit c643451a): the disk-only fix narrowed but didn't close the race — two SwarmAI processes (daemon + CLI eval) could still interleave re-read→merge→rename. Closed by activating utils/file_lock.py (cross-platform flock helpers that had ZERO callers — built and never wired). Activating existing infra beat writing new lock code: fewer lines, Windows msvcrt fallback free (STEERING #2/#3). Two points that mattered: (1) the lock must span the WHOLE read-modify-write (acquire before the disk re-read, release after the rename) — locking only the write leaves TOCTOU open; extracted the body to _merge_and_write() so the lock wraps it cleanly. (2) The RED test holds the sidecar lock from a background fd and asserts persist BLOCKS until release — a behavioral test of mutual exclusion, not a "lock object exists" check. Adversarial SHIP but flagged a real MEDIUM: blocking flock acquired while holding the in-process _data_lock = head-of-line-blocking if a peer hangs. Deliberately scoped to a follow-up todo rather than tack on a timeout under fatigue — the timeout fix is itself a design decision (skip-on-timeout reintroduces the data loss; raise fails the op), not a mechanical add. (2026-06-25, run_0fac5a91)
- _persist_golden_set disk-only data-loss fix (run_fb4b42d2, commit 22828615): the merge loop iterated only self._cases, so a case added to golden_set.yaml by ANOTHER session after this process loaded it was silently dropped on the next persist (caused the 13-case corruption incl all GS_TRAJ). Before touching code, the reverse-risk had to be cleared: "won't appending disk-only cases resurrect a deleted one?" — grepped eval_service.py and confirmed delete_case is SOFT (tier=archived, stays in _cases) with NO hard-remove path, so disk-only ALWAYS = externally-added. That single invariant check is what made the fix safe AND simple (append + dedup set), and it's encoded as a loud INVARIANT comment so a future hard-delete path can't silently turn it into a resurrection bug. The bug had ZERO test coverage — a self-authored "merge-preserve" docstring claimed it already prevented this (it only preserved FIELDS on in-both cases, not whole disk-only CASES). Lesson: a function whose docstring describes data-loss PREVENTION is exactly where to demand a RED test that reproduces the loss — the prose was confidently wrong. (2026-06-25, run_fb4b42d2)

The strongest adversarial line (could a stale disk copy of a user-owned field clobber a programmatic update?) was only retired by the sub-agent enumerating EVERY writer of tags/notes/promoted_from and confirming none exist — proof by exhaustion, not assertion. (2026-06-25, run_fb4b42d2)

- **Self-evolution closed loop — remaining 5 items shipped (run_cf491cab, goal profile, 5 cycles)** — M4-3 score-divergence flag, M4-4 seed replayability verification, finding-1 (sibling-committed, verified not re-litigated), M5 closed-loop audit (Goodhart guard + loop-closed meta-test), M3.5 APPLY-gate redesign doc. Key lessons: (1) **Meta-review caught the highest-value defect, not the code adversarial** — the divergence banner fired on has_red() which includes rule-only chronic recurrence (live OPERATIONAL 799x), so it would fire EVERY session forever = banner-blindness. Code review can't catch this (the code is correct); only the operational "what fires in steady-state after 6 months?" lens does. Fix: has_gate_failure() — divergence is event-like (a deployed gate failed), not level-like (anything red). (2) **Pure-function-first made every milestone testable on synthetic state** — compute_score_divergence, audit_recurrence, loop_closed_meta_test are all I/O-free, so divergence/Goodhart/loop-closure are asserted without a live eval run. (3) **My own M5 test caught a Goodhart-ordering bug** — gate_failed masked logged_less because the collapsed-capture denominator must be checked FIRST (you can't trust recurrence counts you stopped logging). (4) **STEERING #7 collision discipline paid off** — finding-1 was committed by a parallel sibling session mid-pipeline (eval_service.py); I verified ownership via git, built ON it, never reverted. (5) Adversarial found 5 (2 CRITICAL: string-score crash, None-entry crash that would make Phase-3d a permanent silent no-op); meta-review found 1 HIGH (banner-blindness). All 6 fixed, 215 tests green. (2026-06-25, run_cf491cab)

- **Recurrence = wrong-layer signal + enforcement-must-be-outside-the-agent — TWO independent confirmations same week.** (1) reconcile-gap recurred 33× because every fix targeted layers 1-9 (persist/SSE/store/reconcile/correlation); the real defect was layer 6 (TabView dual-source render selector), never touched. Fix = single render source (run_9db9f987, commits 70b4108b+e0de50c7). (2) Parallel session: background-command no-timeout hang (the 12-min `find &`, AGENT.md "a hang IS a failure") — CLI #61568 is unfixable at the model/rule layer (Anthropic: "defense comes from outside the agent"; Claude Code provably ignores deny-rules + CLAUDE.md), so the fix is a PreToolUse `background_command_guard` hook that default-denies backgrounding (commit 8bb8b849). BOTH prove the same architecture truth = SOUL "I am the OS not the model" + STEERING #1 (structural prevention > watchdog): when a bug recurs N times, map ALL layers and find the one never touched — and put the enforcement in a layer the model cannot bypass (a hook/gate), never in a rule the model is asked to follow. Meta-debugging heuristic: recurrence count ≈ "you are fixing the wrong layer" counter. (2026-06-25, run_9db9f987 + background_guard session)

- Recovery unification (M1-M5, run_25f4b74c): a pre-built policy ABSTRACTION made 4 trigger migrations near-mechanical. The coordinator already had 7 verdicts + a RecoveryContext superset + dispatch; R3b-e were "add 2 policy classes (BareThreshold, GracefulEscalation) + wire 4 call sites", not redesign. PIT06 in action: GracefulEscalation's base/escalated verdicts are INJECTED, so ONE shape served 3 triggers (streaming-timeout KILL→KILL_HARD, tool-hang INTERRUPT→KILL, OOM KILL→KILL_HARD). When you build the 2nd of N similar things, parameterize the variant and the rest are wiring. (2026-06-25, run_25f4b74c)
- The TOCTOU fix reused DEAD code: self._lock (defined in __init__, never acquired) became the recovery transaction lock. Verify-before-build found it was dead via grep, and the deadlock audit (does _spawn_lock nest with it? do callers hold it?) cleared it BEFORE coding. A dead lock that already exists at the right scope is the cheapest possible fix for a concurrency bug. (2026-06-25, run_25f4b74c)
- Adversarial caught 3 real bugs I introduced across the milestones, each a DIFFERENT class: (1) idempotent guard skipped identity-drop on the give-up path (would revive a doomed --resume); (2) the "single convergence" invariant was overstated — kill() bypassed the lock I added to _crash_to_cold_async; (3) M2 consulted a STALE user_stopped flag that would block a 4h reclamation forever. All three passed the per-milestone tests — the value was in the FINAL integrated cross-path review, not the per-milestone ones. The cross-path question "do two DIFFERENT triggers race the same unit?" is what surfaced the kill()-bypass. (2026-06-25, run_25f4b74c)
- "Skip what's done, don't duplicate" (XG directive) saved a full milestone of wasted work AND a guaranteed collision: M6 (recovery_exhausted card + coordinator auto-resume) was verified pre-built by 7 passing tests (not assumed — ran them); M7 (orphan-reaper + stream-admission) was being committed live by a parallel R6 session (commit e6898d63). Auditing each milestone's success-criterion grep BEFORE building is the cheap discriminator between "todo" and "done". (2026-06-25, run_25f4b74c)

The 5 FAIL docs noise is a missing-date METADATA problem, not staleness — reclaim correctly refuses date-less, so it cannot fix them. When a destructive batch op targets user data, dry-run BEFORE building the apply path; the preview can invalidate the AC. (2026-06-25, run_94fd5597, auto-cultivated)

<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- **A backend-only feature that surfaces through a frontend event needs a FRONTEND test of the event's delivery path — backend unit tests are structurally blind to it (C011 class).** run_d8dce02a (recovery_exhausted): backend emitted the event correctly, 4 backend tests green, but the feature was 100% dead for the foreground tab — the frontend's generation guard discarded the event because it's yielded AFTER `result` (which bumps streamGen). No backend test could ever catch this; the original BUILD had no frontend test for the guard-survival path. Adversarial review found it. Lesson: when a backend change's USER VALUE is realized in the frontend, an AC is not "done" until a frontend test exercises the actual event→render path. The fix added exactly that (fire result→bump, then the event, assert toast raised). (2026-06-25, run_d8dce02a)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- **Side-channel SSE events (toast-only, no store/streamState writes) must be handled BEFORE the cross-turn-bleed generation guard, not after.** The guard exists to drop stale stream content from an interrupted turn — but a session-scoped signal (recovery_exhausted) is semantically true regardless of turn generation, and is emitted post-`result` so it always looks "stale" to the guard. Handle-before-guard + immediate `return` is the correct pattern (mirrors file_changed). Verified safe: bypassing the guard trades a guaranteed missed-toast for an at-most-one (backend high-water-mark gate), self-clearing (session_start backstop), semantically-correct straggler. (2026-06-25, run_d8dce02a)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- **A `git stash push <pathspec>` + `pop` can leave the popped entry's slot occupied by a DIFFERENT pre-existing stash — verify the hash before `stash drop` (STEERING #7).** run_d8dce02a: dropped what I thought was my own redundant stash; it was a parallel session's WIP (hook_builder/streaming_orchestrator/chat.py). Recovered immediately via the printed dropped-hash (`git stash store <hash>`). Lesson: `git stash show <hash>` to confirm contents are yours BEFORE dropping; never assume `stash@{0}` is the one you just created. (2026-06-25, run_d8dce02a)

- Gate-1 SSA earns its keep on resource-arbitration changes: it caught that swapping the spawn_budget penalty input reopens COE05 BEFORE any code was written, by reading the docstring (resource_monitor.py:293-299) that states the alive_count penalty exists precisely to prevent simultaneous-peak jetsam. (2026-06-24, run_6ea35431, auto-cultivated)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 2 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- **R6a (run_6ea35431): the binding constraint was NOT where the design first pointed.** The design's two-limit-split framing (§9.4) implied changing `spawn_budget`'s penalty input (`alive_count`→`streaming_count`). Gate-1 SSA BLOCKED that: `spawn_budget`'s `alive_count` penalty IS the COE05 simultaneous-peak floor (resource_monitor.py:293-299 docstring says so verbatim), and `streaming_count` can't see idle→stream transitions at spawn-time → reopens COE05. The REAL fix was far narrower: the `compute_max_tabs` COUNT CEILING (not the penalty) fired before the budget gate, so removing it from the 3 backend consumers + leaving `spawn_budget` untouched fixed the user pain (eviction on memory-abundant machines) AND preserved the OOM floor. Lesson: when a design names a mechanism to change, verify it's the BINDING constraint before touching it — `spawn_budget` already permitted the 4th tab; only the ceiling blocked it. The penalty-swap was both wrong and unnecessary. (2026-06-24)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- **R6a: every adversarial finding this run was a "the fix is broader/narrower than the old behavior" class — verify symmetry against the OLD code, not just correctness in isolation.** Gate-2 HIGH: a pessimistic retry fallback (`_alive=4` on registry-None) was STRICTER than the old `_alive=0`, wrongly aborting healthy resumes during daemon restart (registry-None ⇒ FEW peers, not a full machine). Gate-2 MEDIUM: a bare `return "ready"` first-tab bypass was BROADER than both the old code and the `_acquire_channel_slot` it claimed to mirror (silent OOM door). Both found only by tracing NEW-vs-OLD divergence per input. The discriminator None-router-vs-raised-exception needed OPPOSITE defaults (0 vs pessimistic) — "can't read the count" is two different facts. (2026-06-24, run_6ea35431)

Re-scoped to P3. Gate 2 (adversarial) then caught that the corrected 1-line fix (`force=True`→`force=False`) WIDENED a pre-existing latent race: wakers now loop on within-grace misses, exposing a lost-wakeup on the shared `_slot_available` Event that could stall a waker up to 300s. Lesson: a "trivial" 1-line flag flip on a CRITICAL concurrency primitive is exactly where adversarial earns its keep — the fix was correct in isolation but changed the loop's *frequency of looping*, turning a benign latent bug reachable. Neither gate was ceremony. (2026-06-21, run_002eca4c)

- **Pipeline v4 "BUILD is the Root Cause" (2026-05-29, commit 75c128df)** — Three structural improvements: (1) Litmus Pre-Gate (Check 8e, 30s pre-screen, PASS/BORDERLINE/FAIL with code enforcement), (2) AC Coverage Matrix (Check 8f, every PLAN AC must map to impl+test+verified, cross-reference with identifier matching), (3) TEST 3-Layer Strategy (Check 8g, BLOCK for full/bugfix when ac_driven not run, cross-artifact count validation). Also: adversarial prompts refocused on bugs not gaming (~30% FP expected reduction), DELIVER inherits REVIEW litmus weak_areas, ac_verification in STAGE_DEPTH, human_override requires reason. Two rounds adversarial per component (litmus: 17 findings, AC: 10 findings, all addressed). +1056 lines, 155 tests (was 114). Root insight: REVIEW/DELIVER were compensating for low BUILD quality — fixing BUILD quality upstream reduces downstream overhead.

- [guideline] **Self-maintaining docs need measurement AND detection — metrics auto-refresh gives false "docs are fresh" confidence while prose descriptions silently drift** — Token budget prose was wrong for 6 days (40K/25K → should be 100K/50K/30K) undetected because metric numbers refreshed daily, creating illusion of freshness. Fix: 6 staleness checks (regex match prose ↔ code patterns) + `--check-staleness` CLI flag. Pattern: any auto-refreshed doc needs BOTH numeric measurement (counters) and semantic detection (assertions). One without the other = partial coverage that feels like full coverage. (2026-05-26, run_0ed138eb)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [guideline] **Registries should be declarative config not Python dicts — "list that grows" never belongs in code** — Replaced hardcoded `engine_checks = { 12 entries }` with `engines.yaml`. Adding a new engine now requires 3 lines of YAML, zero Python knowledge. Same pattern as `dangerous_commands.json` (security), skill manifests (capabilities). Generalized rule: if it's a list that grows without logic changes, extract to config. (2026-05-26, run_0ed138eb)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **Exit code for control flow, never grep output — grep masks crashes AND misses severity variations** — `prod.sh` used `grep -q "STALE"` to check staleness: (1) missed `[MISSING]` severity, (2) if python crashed, traceback doesn't contain "STALE" → grep fails → else-branch says "all good". Same pattern: any `command | grep pattern` for control flow silently passes on left-side crash when pipefail is off. Rule: use exit codes. If the tool doesn't have exit codes for your case, add them. (2026-05-26, adversarial round 1)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **Module-level mutable globals break on 2nd call — "works in isolation" ≠ "works as a library"** — `_SCRIPT_DEADLINE = 0.0` set once on first call, never reset on subsequent import-based calls. Pattern: any module-level mutable state that's set inside a function but read on every invocation will be stale on 2nd+ call. Test: call the entry function twice with different expectations. Fix: always-reset at function entry, or use function-local state. (2026-05-26, adversarial round 1)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **`sorted(path.iterdir())` sorts lexicographically on filenames — UUID-named dirs have NO chronological order** — `run_a3f7b2c1` sorts before `run_f8985057` but may be newer. Any code that needs "most recent" from a directory scan MUST read timestamps from file contents (e.g., `created_at` in run.json), not rely on directory names. Pattern existed in TWO places (`cmd_publish` and `_auto_validate_before_advance`) — same bug, different call sites. (2026-05-26, adversarial round 2)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **Adversarial review on utility scripts catches accumulation bugs invisible to unit tests** — 225-line "simple" script had: cumulative timeout 15x budget (15 commands × 10s each), SQLite connection leak on exception path, missing UTF-8 encoding. None triggerable in unit tests (each test mocks subsystems). Fresh-context reviewer's value: sees the WHOLE script as a single-pass execution with real timing, not isolated components. (2026-05-26, run_0ed138eb)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- [decision] **Boost before truncation, not after — contextual relevance must influence what survives the cutoff** — Initial design applied relation boost AFTER per-type `[:max_count]` truncation. A contextually relevant entry ranked 8th in its type group (max=7) would be eliminated before boost could promote it. Fix: compute boost score first, sort by boosted score, THEN truncate. This is the correct ordering for any "select top-N with contextual signal" pipeline. (2026-05-19, run_a070cc0c)

- [model] **PE-reviewed design doc = mechanical BUILD** — 1,487-line spec (26 PE findings, all fixed) meant every function, schema, and pattern was pre-decided. Zero design decisions during BUILD. Same lesson as Pollinate: "spec-driven development works for AI agents." (2026-05-03, run_7bc1afc5)

- [decision] **Physical path separation is the right architecture for safe updates** -- Both Desktop (app bundle vs ~/.swarm-ai/) and Hive (/opt/swarmai/ vs /home/swarm/.swarm-ai/) use the same pattern: code directory is fully replaceable, user data directory is never touched by updates. This survived 2 live deploys, 1 SSM update test, and 1 manual rsync. AGENT.md and SOUL.md are system-owned (rebuilt from code on startup) — users get new agent behavior automatically, which is the right default. Pattern: any system that separates "what the system does" from "what the user has accumulated" should use physical path separation, not file-level merging. (2026-04-29, Hive update architecture)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Layer versioning on existing detection-based migrations, don't replace** -- DB had 19+ detection-based column migrations (PRAGMA table_info). Instead of rewriting them as versioned, layered PRAGMA user_version on top: Phase 1 (legacy detection) runs only at version=0, Phase 2 (versioned) runs incrementally. Zero migration rewritten, new migrations slot in with `if current < N:`. Returning users hit 1ms fast path (single PRAGMA read). Pattern: when extending a working system, add a version gate above it — don't refactor the working code into the new scheme. (2026-04-28, run_91a6fb7e)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->

- [decision] **Shadow mode validates infrastructure before wiring it live** -- Recall L2/L3 (12,872 indexed chunks) was built 4 weeks ago but never triggered in production. Instead of wiring it directly (and risking low-quality recall polluting agent context), shadow mode logs results without injection. First data point: FTS5-only = 14ms/12 hits, FTS5+embedding = 1827ms/12 hits — same results, 130× slower. Two weeks of shadow data will answer "is FTS5 sufficient?" before committing to a production path. Pattern: for any infrastructure that's built-but-unvalidated, add a shadow/observability layer first. The cost (fire-and-forget task + JSONL append) is negligible; the risk mitigation is significant. (2026-04-14, run_419ac912)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Raw verbatim > LLM summaries for recall (MemPalace validated)** -- 96.6% vs 84.2% on LongMemEval R@5. "Intelligence at read time, not write time." Our 4-layer architecture takes both: curated Brain for fast decisions (L0/L1) + raw Transcript for precision recall (L3). Don't compress what you can search. (2026-04-11, a2b19a5)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Audit before building saves 60% effort** -- Memory Architecture v2 planned 5 phases. Audit found Phases 1-3 already shipped (knowledge_store, recall_engine, distillation). Actual new work: 2 phases instead of 5. Always `grep -r` for existing implementations before creating pipeline runs. (2026-04-11, run_a591cb81)

- [guideline] **4-component session architecture (v7)** -- Replacing a monolithic manager with SessionRouter/SessionUnit/LifecycleManager/SessionRegistry gave clean separation and testability. Each component has a single job.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Filesystem-first for skills and context** -- No database for skills, no database for context files. Filesystem is portable, git-tracked, and human-readable. This choice has never been regretted.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Prevention over recovery** -- Timeouts, state guards, and guaranteed state transitions beat elaborate error handling. Making failure structurally impossible is better than catching it at runtime.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Property-based testing** -- Hypothesis tests catch edge cases that example-based tests miss. Especially valuable for workspace management, project CRUD, and context loading.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Strangler fig for large refactors** -- Running new code alongside old until behavior parity is verified prevents the cascade of bugs that big-bang rewrites cause.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Pipeline dog-fooding validates architecture** -- Using the pipeline to build its own validator (run_7edb81f3) proved the orchestration loop, TDD methodology, and structural enforcement work. Self-validation (validator validates the pipeline that built it) is the strongest trust signal.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **WARN-only checks must not penalize scores** -- The pipeline validator initially reduced checks_passed for warnings (decision not logged, token_cost=0). This misled the agent into thinking something structurally failed. Fix: only BLOCK errors reduce the count.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Sentinel pattern for falsy defaults** -- Python's `x or default` fails for empty collections. The test helper `_stage_record(decisions=[])` got the default decisions instead of empty. Always use a sentinel object for optional-but-falsy parameters.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Git as ground truth for memory** -- DailyActivity now captures actual git commits alongside session text. Distillation verifies implementation claims against git log before promoting to MEMORY.md. Unverified claims get `[UNVERIFIED]` tag instead of confident promotion. COE C005 fix: prevents self-reinforcing false memories.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **JSON file as inter-component data bus** -- `health_findings.json` (hook writes, briefing reads) follows the same proven pattern as `signal_digest.json`. This is the standard integration pattern for Core Engine feedback loops: component A writes structured JSON, component B reads at its own lifecycle phase. No coupling, survives restarts.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **TDD catches naming mismatches early** -- Sprint 3 pipeline: RED phase caught `TodoManager` vs `ToDoManager` and `HookContext` API mismatch before code was written. Without TDD, these would have been runtime production errors.

- [guideline] **Pipeline self-assessment reveals process debt** -- Running the pipeline on itself (run_46c4373d) exposed 10 gaps: skipped validator, incomplete run.json, no REPORT.md, fake review. The pipeline skill is too long for honor-system compliance -- needs structural enforcement (pre-flight checks that block stage advancement).

- [guideline] **Feishu adapter pattern transfers directly to Slack** -- run_4357bd66: The background-thread + call_soon_threadsafe bridge proven with Feishu worked identically for Slack Socket Mode. Same threading model, same error isolation, same gateway integration. When adding future channels (Discord, WeChat, Teams), follow this pattern: sync SDK in background thread, bridge to asyncio main loop. ~300 lines per adapter.

- [guideline] **Checkpoint/resume works for large cross-session builds** -- run_4357bd66: Plan + Think + Evaluate completed in session 1, checkpointed at BUILD. Session 2 resumed, found all code pre-implemented, ran VERIFY phase. Pipeline state in .artifacts/ survived across sessions cleanly. Key: checkpoint reason must be specific ("BUILD requires TDD across 13 files") not vague.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Core Engine: design flywheels together, not as isolated features** -- Designing 6 subsystems as interconnected flywheels (not standalone features) revealed 12 cross-flywheel feedback loops, 5 of which were completely missing. The missing loops were all about learning from outcomes -- the system acted but didn't measure whether the action helped. Identifying this pattern before building prevented the "automation without learning" trap. Key: map the loops FIRST, then build the components.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Core Engine: LLM for judgment, mechanical for checks** -- Using Haiku for weekly memory pruning produces smarter results than any date-based heuristic ($0.15/week). "Is this still relevant?" is a judgment call, not a pattern match. The principle "best outcome, not cheapest path" prevents false economy in background intelligence.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Core Engine: product-level from the start** -- Moving swarm-jobs from workspace scripts to `backend/jobs/` (16 modules, 52 tests) means new users get signal pipeline, scheduler, and system jobs out of the box. Workspace-level code that's essential for the product is tech debt with a ticking clock.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Context file ownership model prevents brain corruption** -- Four explicit categories (system/user/agent/auto-gen) with enforced write access. AGENT.md is system-owned (codebase template, overwritten on startup) -- editing the runtime `.context/` copy is always wrong. Learned the hard way: edits to `.context/AGENT.md` got silently overwritten on restart, twice.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **One health harness, sensible intervals** -- ContextHealthHook does light refresh (every session, <1s) and deep check (daily first session, <10s). Over-engineering (per-file hooks, per-session deep checks) wastes resources. The goal is "in control", not "maximally monitored".
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Read-time fallback covers fragmented write paths** -- MEMORY.md has 5 independent writers (Edit tool, locked_write function, locked_write CLI, direct I/O, memory_extractor). Instead of wiring post-write hooks into each, `context_health_hook` regenerates the memory index on every session end (<10ms). One read-side catch-all beats five write-side patches. Applied for Progressive Memory Disclosure index regeneration (2026-03-31).

The highest-value design review feedback is "your framing is wrong", not "here's a bug."

Both wired in-pipeline before delivery. Previous pipeline (Progressive Disclosure) shipped the same class of bug undetected. Integration trace is now structural enforcement, not honor-system.

- [decision] **Read-side embedding sync follows proven pattern** -- MEMORY.md has 5 independent writers. Instead of wiring post-write hooks into each (fragile), embedding sync runs in context_health_hook after index regeneration — same "one read-side catch-all" pattern that works for index regen. Pattern: when N writers exist, put the sync on the single read/refresh path.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **Self-E2E-review after pipeline catches integration gaps** -- Pipeline completed 8 stages with 10/10 confidence, then E2E review found 2 core features disconnected (index never persisted, keyword matching always empty). Unit tests prove components work; only E2E trace proves they're wired together. Now a mandatory post-pipeline step.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [guideline] **PermissionError is not ProcessLookupError** -- `os.kill(pid, 0)` raises `PermissionError` (subclass of `OSError`) for processes owned by other users. Catching `(OSError, ProcessLookupError)` treats alive-but-unowned processes as dead. Fix: catch `PermissionError` separately and treat as "alive". This matters for daemon conflict checks where the daemon runs as the same user but PID 1 (launchd) owns the port check socket. Pattern: any PID liveness check must handle 3 states: alive+ours, alive+theirs (PermissionError), dead (ProcessLookupError).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **boot_id beats poll frequency for restart detection** -- A 10s health poll misses a daemon restart that completes in <10s. Adding a `boot_id` (random string generated at startup) to the health response lets the watchdog detect silent restarts without reducing poll frequency to sub-second. The watchdog compares boot_id across polls — change means restart even if health never went unhealthy. This is the standard pattern for detecting process identity changes across restarts.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Single source of truth for test execution** -- pyproject.toml `addopts = "-n 4"` is the ONLY place that controls xdist worker count. conftest.py only downgrades (memory pressure) or overrides bypasses (agent passing `-n 0`). Makefile has zero `-n` logic — it delegates entirely to pyproject.toml. One config, one enforcement point, zero escape hatches. Arrived at this after 12 commits of doing it wrong (runtime injection, multiple entry points, silent fallbacks).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Loud fallbacks prevent invisible degradation** -- Every xdist override path in conftest.py prints to stderr with `⚠️` or `✓`. For 5+ days, tests silently ran serial (no xdist) because every fallback was `except: pass` or `if not hasattr: return`. Nobody noticed. The print-to-stderr pattern costs nothing and makes every test run self-documenting.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **Agent behavioral constraints are system constraints** -- The agent generates pytest commands. Without an explicit rule in AGENT.md ("never pass `-n 0`"), the agent's self-healing behavior (crash → disable xdist) bypassed 12 layers of conftest defense. Constraining the agent's command generation was more effective than all the runtime guards combined.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [decision] **Ship heuristic first, layer ML later** -- Evolution Optimizer built with correction-pattern heuristics (zero dependencies) and DSPy-ready interface. The heuristic handles the common case ("don't X" → remove, "use Y" → add) at near-zero cost. GEPA/DSPy is an additive enhancement, not a prerequisite. This shipped Phase 3 in one session instead of blocking on pip install dspy. (2026-04-10, run_8266f10b)

- [guideline] **Anti-rationalization tables at decision points (Superpowers pattern)** -- Adopted from obra/superpowers (222K stars). Embed pre-emptive rationalization-refutation tables BEFORE the critical decision point in each pipeline stage doc (BUILD, REVIEW, TEST, DELIVER). Per O003: enforcement text at decision point, not file end. Each row names an exact thought the agent is having + why it's wrong + evidence citation. Tables are 6-8 rows (within LLM attention budget). ### Architecture Decision Records

**ADR: code-intel v3 internal (non-HTTP) subsystems anchor their domain-flow to a real RELATED route, not a synthetic symbol-flow** (2026-07-18, run_89e28075)
Context: 6 gap subsystems (context-memory, self-evolution, pipeline, proactive-intelligence, channels-runtime, services-jobs) are mostly internal — they have few or no HTTP routes. To make them "covered" domains they need a flow, but `finalize_v3` fail-closed requires every `flow.entry_ref` to resolve to a real `route.id` (a symbol/file-path entry_ref is rejected as dangling). Decision: each internal subsystem's flow anchors at the single most-representative REAL route that exercises it (services-jobs→`POST /api/jobs/run`, pipeline→`POST /api/artifacts/pipeline/advance`, self-evolution→`POST /api/escalations/{project}/{id}/resolve`, context-memory→`/api/memory/save-session`+`/api/recall/coverage`, proactive→`GET /api/system/briefing`, channels-runtime→`POST /api/channels/{id}/start`) + carries the subsystem's real business_rules with verified file:line anchors. Rejected alternatives: (a) synthetic `entry_type=symbol` flows with a `file::Symbol` entry_ref — REJECTED by the fail-closed gate (doesn't resolve to a route.id), and rightly so: it would let a domain claim coverage with no real entry point; (b) flowless domains + teaching `compute_subsystem_coverage` to credit `business_rules[].anchor` as evidence — viable but weakens the coverage signal (a domain could claim coverage by naming a file it never enters). Why it's hard to reverse: it sets the pattern for how EVERY future internal subsystem enters the v3 domain layer, and the route-anchor is what both the accounting gate AND `compute_subsystem_coverage` key off — changing it means re-authoring all internal domains + the coverage mechanism. The route-anchor is truthful (those routes DO exercise the subsystem) AND doubles as the accounting-backfill for the 4.8%→100% first-order gap.

**ADR: Socratic method in the autonomous pipeline = interrogate the spec/framing, NOT ask the user more** (2026-06-26, run_932c0991)
Context: Amazon PR/FAQ and the AWS sample-ai-plc / aidlc-workflows repos use Socratic, gated questioning to elicit requirements — but they are human-in-the-loop IDE assistants where "Socratic" means writing questions into a file and BLOCKING for the user's answer. SwarmAI's pipeline is autonomous. Decision: the correct port of the Socratic method to an autonomous pipeline is a *self-Socratic* micro-loop — the stage re-scans its OWN clarification output (EVALUATE WHO/WHAT/WHY/WHEN; THINK risk-probe assumptions) for ambiguity/hedge terms and self-answers each hit by reading code/DDD, escalating to the user ONLY what is genuinely unknowable. This is the same philosophy as the just-shipped Understanding Gate ("the claim must survive a skeptic's refutation") shifted LEFT and narrowed to the requirement-clarification layer — interrogate the framing before building, not after. Rejected alternatives: (a) the interactive ask-the-user grill protocol — already downgraded once because it was "almost always skipped" (think.md), and human-blocking gates become rubber stamps (aidlc #366/#367 ceremonial-gate bugs); (b) full PR/FAQ Working-Backwards — valuable but only for greenfield product framing, deferred as a greenfield-only follow-up (plan B). Why it's hard to reverse: it sets the questioning philosophy for every future EVALUATE/THINK and is validator-enforced (`_check_ambiguity_scan`), so changing it means re-teaching the gate and re-cutting the stage docs. The enforcement parallels the Understanding Gate exactly (strict-require / relaxed-exempt / distinct error tag), so the two form one coherent "diagnose-before-build" family rather than two competing mechanisms.

- [decision] **Projected-but-not-injected reference files = zero-cost shared context** -- CONTEXT.md (ubiquitous language glossary) needed to be available to the agent but NOT in every session's system prompt (wasteful for non-skill work). Solution: `_SYSTEM_REFERENCE_FILES` in `ensure_directory()` — copied to `.context/` on startup (always-overwrite from template) but not in `CONTEXT_FILES` list (no prompt injection). Same pattern as `_AGENT_MANAGED_FILES` but system-owned instead of agent-owned. Reusable for any future reference doc (CONVENTIONS.md, GLOSSARY.md, etc.) that should be on-demand, not always-on. Same session, same person: I authored the gate AND the blind spot. The adversarial also found a HIGH the whole TDD cycle missed — `standard` profile (a documented rank-4 alias for full) fell through both my strict/relaxed sets → gate silently dead on the most common legacy strict profile. Both are CLASS_A authorship-trap instances: I had perfect understanding of intent and shipped coverage gaps anyway. Fixes: fail-closed default (strict unless explicitly relaxed — closes standard + all unknowns in one move) + widened M1 verbs/gerunds/imperative + `_strip_negated_verbs` (so "does not implement X" present-state claims don't false-block). Lesson reconfirmed: a parametrized test that only covers the profiles YOU listed (`full/bugfix/goal`) silently omits the one that matters (`standard`); adversarial breadth > author breadth. R1 non-negotiable, again.

Trap = efficient-but-wrong framing with NO protective cue; PASS requires the agent to self-initiate the protective read AND cite the governing rule by name (not just "advise caution" — that's breadcrumb-following). - [pitfall] **Self-review covers self-assumptions, not reality — adversarial is the only fix (2026-06-25, run_0305426d)** — Shipped a HIGH bug into REVIEW that BUILD + REVIEW + my OWN SMOKE test all passed: I claimed "draft skeletons are excluded from the eval score" and wrote a SMOKE proving it — but my SMOKE only exercised the DEFAULT path (where the `eval_method=behavior` filter drops them). It never tested the `behavior_trajectory`-tag path that BYPASSES that filter, where an unrefined skeleton WOULD spawn a real agent and fold a tautological "read-the-doc" pass into the score. `eval_trajectory_capture` had no `tier=draft` guard. The fresh adversarial Gate-2 agent's 7-vector attack found it. Lesson: a self-authored test inherits the author's blind spot — it covers what I ASSUMED, never what I missed. My confidence ("exclusion is unconditional") was the counter-signal; it was conditional. Also: **honesty must live in CODE, not prose** — the skeleton self-marked as a placeholder in its title/rubric text, but the runner never reads prose; the fix was a `tier=draft` code skip. Reconfirms why Gate 2 is non-negotiable (R1).

- **Session Lifecycle Resilience (2026-06-17, 3 pipelines)** — Invisible self-healing achieved. Key lessons: (1) Detection separated from action = testable HealthSensor. (2) "One more turn" graceful trigger (max-20) beats immediate kill. (3) Frontend 30s HEAL_GRACE_PERIOD eliminates false error toasts. (4) Unified `_arm_recovery_checkpoint` for all involuntary kill paths (DRY > per-path recovery). (5) User-stop absolute priority — `_user_stopped_current_turn` persists until next send(). COE10 止血: SWARMAI_SELF_HEAL env gate prevented damage while hardening recovery chain.

- **MessageStore Single-Writer (2026-06-17, 5 pipelines)** — 45 setMessages → 1 store. Key lessons: (1) Phase machine (idle/streaming) makes dangerous ops structurally impossible during streaming, not just "checked at call sites". (2) Eager store creation eliminates entire class of fallback code. (3) `endStreaming()` flush semantics — any replace() after needs _reconcileGen++. (4) Strict cross-tab isolation: NEVER use runtime state (streaming/idle) to bypass identity checks (tab ownership). The `store.phase !== 'streaming'` escape hatch CAUSED the cross-tab message leak it was meant to prevent. (5) "Read paths are also write paths" — restoring messages without seeding store IS a store consumer (COE10 bug #6).

- **DDD & Memory Auto-Refresh (2026-06-17, 5 pipelines)** — Rewrite gap closed. Key lessons: (1) Append-only cultivation ≠ content freshness — REWRITE is a separate problem requiring separate infrastructure. (2) Context-word filtering essential — bare regex `\d+-stage` matches unrelated content. (3) Citation verification is the hard gate (not confidence thresholds) against introducing False. (4) Budget-capped LLM calls (3/week, 50K each) keep cost negligible (~$0.50/week). (5) "不引入 False，不容忍 Stale，接受 Imperfect" — False > Stale > Imperfect priority ordering prevents both over-correction and under-correction.

- **E2E Verification Chain + Deploy Unification (2026-06-17, 3 pipelines)** — Three-layer automated verification. Key lessons: (1) No Playwright/Cypress — regressions are logic/state-machine, not visual. (2) Fixture-based contract tests give 90% of integration confidence at 0% of cost. (3) Auto-scope detection via `git diff $deployed_hash..HEAD` makes deploy intelligent. (4) Canary as scheduled job (not manual step) provides continuous validation without human discipline. (5) daemon `.version` git hash is the keystone for deploy auto-scope — without it, can't diff.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Streaming Interruption Fix + Unified Persist (2026-06-18, 2 pipelines)** — Two bugs fixed + knowledge system unified. Key design decisions: (1) **Queue-before-force: eviction cost >> queue cost (100:1 asymmetry)** — 800K token context lost vs 60s wait. Grace period is meaningless if upstream callers immediately escalate to force=True. Grep ALL callers of bypass flags when adding protection mechanisms. (2) **Continue button always-visible > heuristic-triggered** — zero false negatives, zero complexity, matches Claude Code /continue. Users who don't need it ignore it (same as Copy button). (3) **Knowledge routing: content-type determines destination** — "换个项目还有用吗？" YES→MEMORY.md, NO→Projects/<X>/DDD. Not where the user is typing, but what the content IS. (4) **Governance boundary = target file judgment** — if it would change SOUL/AGENT/STEERING = governance (→ s_self-evolution). Everything else = knowledge (→ s_persist). Two-part detection (action+target keywords) prevents "standing rule: prefer atomic writes" false positive. (5) **One schema two consumers** — `persist_routing.py` ROUTING_TABLE consumed by both auto hooks (ddd_cultivation) and manual skill (s_persist). Deriving SAFE_APPEND_SECTIONS from the table eliminates drift. Never maintain parallel classification logic.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Session Unit Strangler-Fig Extraction (2026-06-18, 6 pipelines)** — God module tamed: 4167→2802 lines (−33%). Key decisions & lessons: (1) **Facade-first proves interface cheaply** — 125 LOC facade investment validated the boundary before moving 890+530 LOC. Wrong interface discovered at 1% of the cost of wrong migration. (2) **`self.`→`self._parent.` mechanical transformation** — zero-creativity refactoring. 176 field accesses changed with find-replace, no logic decisions. (3) **Lazy imports break circular deps** — `from .session_unit import SessionState` inside method body avoids import-time cycle. (4) **Removed `__slots__` to enable `mock.patch.object`** — testability > 64 bytes memory savings per instance. (5) **Stubs retained where external callers exist** — `_read_formatted_response` stub stays because `continue_with_permission` + 4 test files call it directly; removing forces 6-file update for zero value. (6) **Bug localization is the real win** — "which of 3 files?" replaces "which of 4167 lines?" for every session bug. Recovery path test baseline (70 tests) locked before any extraction started — STEERING #4 compliance.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Frontend Streaming State Machine (2026-06-18, 5 pipelines P1-P5)** — Explicit 11-mode reducer replaces 18 implicit boolean flags. Key decisions & lessons: (1) **Matrix testing is O(N×M) complete** — 11 modes × 20 events = 300 tests. Covers every transition including "invalid event in this mode = no-op." No boolean combination testing could achieve this coverage. (2) **Shadow mode coexistence is correct architecture** — boolean flags AND state machine updated by same single-writer (`setIsStreaming`). Both stay in sync by construction. Removal of booleans is cosmetic (P6 DEFER was correct). (3) **`pendingStreamTabs` provides React re-render trigger** — `tabMapRef` is a `useRef<Map>`, mutations don't re-render. The useState Set is the ONLY mechanism for React to know streaming started. Cannot remove without alternative. (4) **Per-tab state machine on UnifiedTab** — each tab independently tracks mode. Multi-tab isolation achieved without global reducer. (5) **Pure reducer = specification** — `streamingReducer` IS the behavioral spec. Future regression = "reducer allows this transition but shouldn't" or "reducer blocks this transition but shouldn't." Testable at <1ms per case. (6) **DEFER > cosmetic refactor** — P6 (remove 30 boolean reads) was correctly deferred. Zero behavioral gain, non-trivial regression risk, re-render trigger dependency makes it architecturally blocked. Design: `Knowledge/Designs/2026-06-18-usechatstreaminglifecycle-decomposition-design.md`. Commits: 907e2890, ef9371f7, 46d14334, 03130ddc, 7e2307f6.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **SSE Streaming Reliability — State Desync Complete Fix (2026-06-20, 2 pipelines + follow-up)** — The "streaming truncates mid-content" saga fully resolved across 4 commits. Three independent mechanisms were conspiring to kill active streams:

  **(A) Backend streaming-state endpoint lying (a0589e95 + eae7fa0a)**
  `_recover_streaming_on_disconnect` transitions STREAMING→IDLE on SSE disconnect (correct for send() path), but `streaming-state` endpoint reported `streaming=false` based on enum alone. Frontend 15s reconcile poller saw idle → force-cleared. Fix: `is_generating_after_disconnect` property uses persistent flag + `os.kill(pid, 0)` PID liveness check. Unbounded by any timer — clears only when subprocess actually dies, user sends new msg, or kill/cleanup runs. Adversarial found the ship-blocker: initial approach using `_pipe_flush_task.done()` had 30s timeout gap (same bug deferred).

  **(B) Frontend reconcile killing active states (d82dce5c + ee360da6)**
  `waiting_input` (permission prompt pending) was treated identically to `idle` — reconcile force-cleared it. Fix: `ACTIVE_BACKEND_STATES` set guard skips `waiting_input` and `streaming` states. Time-capped at 120min to prevent infinite stuck state if waiting_input SSE event was truly lost.

  **(C) Eviction killing post-disconnect generating sessions (ee360da6)**
  Same-source bug as (A): eviction's `idle_units` filter checked only `state==IDLE`, which includes sessions with `is_generating_after_disconnect=True`. High-RSS sessions (1M context) were eviction target #1. Fix: added `and not u.is_generating_after_disconnect` to filter.

  **Key Design Decisions:**
  1. **Flag+PID liveness > task-duration detection** — `_pipe_flush_task.done()` is bounded by 30s timeout. Flag persists until subprocess actually dies. PID check via `os.kill(pid, 0)` is the only truth source for "is this process alive?"
  2. **State endpoint must report REALITY not enum** — endpoint consumers (frontend reconcile, eviction) make destructive decisions based on the response. Reporting enum value when reality differs = feeding lies to decision-makers.
  3. **Scoped immunity must have BOTH trigger-scope AND time-cap** — `waiting_input` immunity is correct but unbounded immunity = no safety net. 120min cap preserves the reconcile's role as last-resort cleanup.
  4. **Same-source bugs propagate to all consumers of the lying signal** — fixing the endpoint (A) wasn't enough because eviction (C) read `state` directly, bypassing the endpoint. Rule: when fixing a "lying signal", grep ALL consumers of that signal.

  **Avoided Pitfalls:**
  - NOT keeping STREAMING state on disconnect (would break send() which needs IDLE path)
  - NOT adding a timer-based grace period (timers always have a gap; PID liveness is definitive)
  - NOT killing subprocess on flush timeout (output persists to DB for reconciliation recovery)

  Commits: a0589e95, eae7fa0a, d82dce5c, ee360da6. Files: session_unit.py, chat.py, session_router.py, useChatStreamingLifecycle.ts. Tests: 165 backend + tsc clean frontend.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

## What to Watch For
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- [guideline] **A self-hosting tool silently hardcodes its OWN dogfooding stack as the assumed universal — and "make it neutral" is a QUALITY refactor (label every stack-specific example + read the project's declaration), NOT a bugfix (nothing is "broken"; it just quietly only serves its author).** (2026-08-16, run_66d5c01e — de-SwarmAI-izing the Autonomous Pipeline for publish). SwarmAI's pipeline stage docs defaulted every test/build/path example to pytest/npm/backend-core because that's what SwarmAI-self uses; a Go/Java project wouldn't *fail* (the doc-sync GAP is WARN not BLOCK, an unmatched grep returns empty), it would just be led to mimic SwarmAI — a publish-blocker, not a crash. Key moves that generalize to ANY "make our tool work for others" task: **(1) the neutral input already exists — find it before inventing one.** Here every project's TECH.md already had `## Stack`/`## Dev Commands`/`## Codebase Location`/`## Key Subsystems` (uniform across SwarmAI + all sibling DDD projects), so the fix was "read the existing declaration," NOT a new `.project.json` schema (Gate-0 killed that as C042 over-design — a second declaration door for data one door already holds, P8). **(2) Non-regression for the author is the load-bearing AC:** gate the neutralized check on the project *declaring* the convention (SwarmAI has `## Key Subsystems` → its check runs byte-identical; a project without → WARN-skip N/A) — so the tool's own behavior is provably unchanged while others get graceful degradation. **(3) demote, don't delete:** SwarmAI-specific guidance with real value (xdist-deadlock/SWARMAI_SUITE warnings) stays as a labeled "SwarmAI-SELF note," never stripped. **(4) CRITICAL Gate-2 catch — neutralizing an EXECUTABLE block by injecting `<placeholder>` prose into runnable code is a silent no-op:** the first draft wrote `CORE_PATH='<from TECH.md — SwarmAI: ^backend/core/>'` inside a real bash block → `grep "$CORE_PATH"` matches nothing → SwarmAI's own doc-sync silently passes even when a real core file was added = a regression *introduced by the neutralization*. Fix = keep the block genuinely runnable: a concrete default (the author's real value) + an `if [ -n "$VAR" ]` skip-guard (empty = other-project-skips), never a descriptive placeholder where the shell expects a value. Tell: if "make this generic" turns a runnable command into one carrying `<...>` tokens in an executed position, you've swapped a working default for a broken template — provide default+guard instead. (Verified: `bash -n` + live-run with SwarmAI defaults matches `backend/core/`; 6 tests mutation-proven.)
- [pitfall] **Caching a "session-constant" value: the correct invalidation boundary is WHICH PATH CONSUMES the value, not "did the content change" — and the gate must key on the CONSUMER's exact complement, never a近似 proxy.** (2026-08-16, run_1dc710db — per-session system-prompt cache). A system prompt is reused on a warm turn but re-consumed on a spawn; the cache is only *safe to reuse* on the turn where the value is DISCARDED (warm reuse → `client.query()` sends only the user message → `options.system_prompt` unused), and MUST be rebuilt on any turn where it is CONSUMED (`_spawn()`). Two traps that made this a HIGH: **(1) gating on a近似 signal instead of the consumer's complement.** The obvious "is this a resume?" proxy (`is_cold_resume` / `needs_context_injection`) requires `_sdk_session_id is None` — but recovery kills (RSS/OOM, self-heal, watchdog, zombie, SSE-disconnect) deliberately PRESERVE `_sdk_session_id` (to respawn with `--resume`). So the exact turn that most needs a fresh build — an eviction/crash **respawn** — is classified "not a resume" → wrongly served the stale cache → the fresh subprocess gets turn-1's briefing/DailyActivity/open-file. The gate must key on the true reuse predicate (`state==IDLE AND _client AND _last_turn_clean` — the complement of every spawn path), not a proxy that happens to correlate. **(2) the backup channel had the same state assumption.** UI-state normally has a fallback delivery (`_prepend_ui_state_to_query` on the query channel) — but it's gated on `state==IDLE`, and a respawn turn is COLD, so BOTH the cached-prompt path and the fallback path fail on the same turn. **Durable tells:** (a) before caching anything "constant," ask "on which turns is this value actually READ downstream?" and invalidate on exactly those — a value discarded on the common path but consumed on a rare recovery path is the danger; (b) a recovery/respawn path that preserves an id to look like continuity will defeat any "is-this-fresh-start" heuristic keyed on that id's absence — enumerate EVERY spawn trigger (cold entry / mid-stream retry / eviction respawn / crash respawn / cold resume / channel resume) and confirm the gate covers all; (c) extract the cache decision into a PURE helper and mutation-test it (the router-inline gate is where the bug lived and had zero coverage until the helper existed). check_freshness/build_system_prompt were left untouched — the fix belongs at the consumer gate, not the producer.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **"这个改动带出一个必须先修的配套" — 先证实那个配套是真问题,再修;一个被当成"前置依赖"的任务常常是基于错误心智模型虚构的。** (2026-08-16, run_1dc710db 收尾). warm-skip(warm 轮跳过 system-prompt 组装)看似"破坏了" `build_session_briefing` 里寄生的 pipeline auto-resume,于是登记了一个"解耦到 scheduler tick"的必修配套 todo。逐源码证实后,这个配套是**假问题**,不该做:**(1) auto-resume 是两半 —— mutation(glob+重写 run.json+增 resume_attempts)+ DIRECTIVE(注入 system-prompt 的文字指令,agent 读到才执行)。执行侧(DIRECTIVE)只在 prompt 真进 CLI 时到达 agent = 只有 cold spawn 轮;warm 轮那份 prompt 本来就被丢,所以 auto-resume 的执行在 warm 轮从来就不工作 —— warm-skip 没破坏任何"曾经工作"的能力。(2) 真正需要 resume 的场景(崩溃/OOM/孤儿)本来就把 session 踢成 COLD → 下一条消息是 cold spawn → cold 轮仍全量组装,mutation+DIRECTIVE 照跑照执行。auto-resume 的兜底核心场景完全没受影响。(3) cooldown 用 `original_updated_at`(pre-mutation 时间戳)判定,跳过几轮 mutation 不污染计时、不让 attempts 永久卡 —— 是"延迟到下次 spawn",不是"永久失能"。(4) 唯一真实窗口:一个用户正活跃对话、纯 warm、从不 spawn 的 session 里恰好有暂停 pipeline —— 但这种"活跃且没崩"的 session 里的暂停多是 Gate-BLOCK/等人决策型,而那种源码明确永不 auto-resume(proactive_intelligence.py:1179-1184 → AWAITING DECISION)。**独立的 pre-existing tech-debt(与 warm-skip 无关,别再当成 warm-skip 的前置):** proactive_intelligence.py:1518 的 `⚠️ TECH-DEBT` 注释("组装 prompt 不该 mutate 状态,该挪到 scheduler tick")是"system-prompt 纯洁性"洁癖诉求,且 run_072d9ef7 已否决"挪 DIRECTIVE 到 tick"(tick 无 agent-session,执行不了文字指令);只有 mutation(纯 fs 写)理论上可挪 tick,但那是独立的洁癖重构,不是任何功能的必修前置。**Durable tell:当一个改动让你觉得"必须先修 X 才能上线",先问"不修 X 到底丢了什么真实能力" —— 用源码证实"丢的是延迟还是能力",别把一个可自愈的延迟当成 blocking 前置去造机制(P9 + C042)。**

- [pitfall] **`adversarial_commit_gate`'s `reviewed_paths` is computed from `git diff --name-only HEAD`, which OMITS untracked files — so a NEW file (e.g. a brand-new test) reviewed by the Gate-2 sub-agent is NOT in the marker's coverage, and the commit is DENIED even though the review genuinely happened.** (2026-08-16, run_864c23b4). Chain: `runtime_hooks._reviewed_paths_at_head` runs `git -C <root> diff --name-only HEAD` at SubagentStop; an untracked new file does not appear in that diff, so it's absent from `reviewed_paths`; the gate then flags exactly that one file as "uncovered path" and DENIES. The adversarial sub-agent HAD read + mutation-tested the file — this is a marker-recording blindspot, not a skipped review. **Fix that works (verified): `git add` the new file FIRST (staging makes it appear in `git diff --name-only HEAD`), THEN spawn the adversarial sub-agent — its SubagentStop marker now captures both the modified and the new file.** Two failing non-fixes: (a) inline `SWARM_ADVERSARIAL_GATE_FORCE=1 git commit …` and even `export`-ing it in the tool shell did NOT bypass — the gate reads env in the DAEMON process, not your Bash subshell's env (known TOOLS/MEMORY pitfall: the inline prefix "isn't reliably seen"); (b) a heredoc + trailing `&& echo`/`git log` on the commit line makes the gate's shlex-split mis-parse those tokens as pathspec → spurious "uncovered path: …/echo" denials — commit with `git commit -qF /tmp/msg.txt` and ZERO extra tokens on the line. **Durable tell: when the adversarial gate denies a commit citing a single NEW file as uncovered, the review probably ran — stage the new file and re-run one confirming adversarial pass so the marker's `git diff HEAD` sees it, rather than reaching for FORCE.** (Sibling of the "stage before you rely on git-diff-HEAD" family.)

- [guideline] **The churn-source fix for an unbounded "re-emit an event a downstream guard then no-ops" loop is to hoist the SAME ownership/eligibility oracle the downstream already uses UP to the EMIT site — don't patch the detector.** (2026-08-16, run_864c23b4). `context_health_hook._refresh_code_intel`'s emit branch fired `code_intel_full_reindex` every health tick for a worktree:null DDD (IVTHub — no local repo, `graph_meta` lacks `repo_root`, so `check_freshness` returns `suggest_full_rebuild=True` forever); the reindex handler then no-op'd each emit (`unowned_repo`) → re-emit next tick → CPU/log/scheduler churn. The tempting fix — change `check_freshness` to return `stale=False` for repo_root-absent — was skeptic-proven HAZARDOUS: `recall_multi` depends on `repo_root`-absent → `stale=True` to stamp code-graph hits as needs-verify; flipping it marks an unverifiable snapshot as fresh. Root fix = gate the emit on `resolve_owned_repo_root(project_dir) is None` (the exact oracle the incremental branch + reindex handler already use), so an unownable project is skipped BEFORE the emit. **Durable tells: (a) when an emitter and its consumer disagree about eligibility (emit fires, consumer no-ops), the fix belongs at the EMITTER using the consumer's own eligibility check — not at a shared detector the emit merely reads; (b) `stale`/`fresh`-type flags are often consumed by MULTIPLE readers with opposite needs (a reindex-scheduler vs a recall-trust-stamp) — never repurpose such a flag's semantics to fix one consumer without auditing the others (P8 one-brain-many-doors, R27 all-consumers).** Also: isolating the current zombie instance (rename `.corrupt-<ts>`, never unlink — STEERING #20) is safe because both reindex loops are `db_path.exists()`-gated and no path recreates a db for a project lacking one (verified: only creator requires db-exists + resolvable local repo). (2026-08-16, run_864c23b4)

- [pitfall] **A self-audit of your own pipeline is only as trustworthy as the adversarial gate on its FIXES — and your green test suite is the LEAST trustworthy part.** (2026-08-11, run_57929039 goal). A 5-agent parallel read produced 13 audit leads; the adversarial gate on the fixes then caught THREE that would have made the pipeline WORSE: (1) the "give the adversarial gate teeth" fix (require a run-scoped `_adv_` marker at completion) would have HARD-BLOCKED every real full/bugfix completion — the SubagentStop hook only has `sdk_session_id`, NOT the pipeline run_id (`_active_pipeline_run_id` is referenced in `runtime_hooks.py` but never SET anywhere), and ts-window scoping instead lets a concurrent sibling run's marker satisfy the gate → DEFERRED (safe teeth need run_id plumbed into the hook first). (2) a non-dict-rejection branch (harden goal_cycle's `adversarial_review` shape) would have false-rejected EVERY valid build publish, because `STAGE_DEPTH["build"]["ac_coverage"]=[]` is a presence-only entry and `ac_coverage` is legitimately a LIST → fixed by guarding on non-empty `child_fields`. (3) the gitignored-project Canvas-surface fallback (emit rows from `files_touched`) would have leaked any file the agent merely READ — `files_touched` is READ-inclusive (`_TRACKED_TOOLS`), absolute + unscoped, incl secrets under `$HOME` → fixed with workspace-containment. **Durable tells:** (a) the green suite MISSED both the marker-teeth and ac_coverage bugs because the tests exercised the WRONG code path (`validate()` completion vs `validate_artifact_data()` publish) and injected the marker with the real run_id the production hook never writes — the O009 "fixture encodes my assumption" trap; every adversarial that caught a bug did so by feeding the REAL production input, not the fixture. When hardening a validator, ask "which of the two gates (publish vs completion) actually runs this, and does my test hit THAT one?" (b) findings are LEADS (PRI14): two audit claims were refuted against source and dropped — a "trivial-profile drops plan" contradiction was phantom, and a "3-way push_ready desync" was phantom for a top-level key no consumer reads. (c) DEFER-with-evidence ≠ evasion — two fixes (the marker teeth + moving the briefing hot-path mutation to cleanup-orphans) were deferred because independent gates proved each unsafe in the CURRENT architecture (the latter would kill crash auto-recovery), not to dodge work. Provenance: run_57929039, commits 3b92703a/1961f202/ab50fcf4/ea92e66f/0786e4bc.

- [pitfall] **Dedup on the brain has two non-obvious constraints — get either wrong and dedup silently becomes a permanent no-op OR deletes the survivor** (2026-08-08, run_2816ab1c, `reclaim_duplicate_entries`). **(a) STRIP by line-precise identity, NOT (title,section).** `content_signature` is title-INCLUSIVE, so two same-signature dups necessarily share `(title, section)` → the shared `_strip_entries` (title,section) set-strip would remove the SURVIVOR too. The old parity-with-reclaim ambiguous-(title,section) guard "solved" that by SKIPPING every ambiguous group — which is every real dup — making dedup a permanent no-op. Fix = `_strip_entries_by_line(content, {e.line_number})` (0-based line index is the only handle that separates same-title dups). **(b) PROTECT by TYPE only, NOT full `is_keep_class`.** An exact-dup is safe to remove in ANY section (the survivor keeps the content), so evergreen-SECTION must gate AGE-decay only — never dedup. Using full `is_keep_class` (which includes evergreen sections) made dedup a no-op on the fully-evergreen EVOLUTION, the exact file that most needed it. `_dedup_protected` = the 4 judgment types (principle/correction/decision/model) + COE only. Gate-2 also caught a `line_number > 0` filter that would skip a line-0 dup (unreachable in practice — DDD files start with `## ` — but invariant-weakening; removed + regression-tested).

- [pitfall] **A bug can be BORN from two separate runs' fixes INTERACTING — diff-scoped adversarial (each run reviews only its own diff) structurally cannot see it; audit the COMPOSED final state across all runs that touched the subsystem as ONE artifact.** Canvas Layer-2 was built over two pipeline runs (run_0df821e0 base + run_bfbbe0fd hardening). Each run's adversarial gate passed on its own diff. An integration audit of the COMPOSED code then found a lying comment (RP48) created by the fixes' interaction: run_bfbbe0fd's in-flight gate sits in `publish_file_event`->`resolve` and drops tool-closed writes BEFORE the enqueue, which made run_bfbbe0fd's own final-drain comment ("catches tool-closed end-of-turn writes") FALSE — the queue is empty for those, so only the finish-sweep surfaces them. Neither run's diff-review could catch it because the contradiction spans two diffs. **Durable tells:** (a) after N changes land on one subsystem across multiple runs, do a WHOLE-SYSTEM read of the final composed state — treat the union as a new artifact a fresh reader audits, not N verified diffs; (b) the signature here was a comment claiming a SCOPE/GUARANTEE that a SIBLING fix silently invalidated — grep every mechanism's scope-claim against the OTHER fixes' behavior; (c) external post-ship E2E review is the layer that catches this class — the base run passed its full pipeline adversarial, then an external review of the SHIPPED code found 2 real gaps + 1 overclaim the diff-review never saw. Green-pipeline != audited-as-a-whole. Verified empirically (tool-closed->dropped-at-gate->not-in-queue), fixed in commit aaf10626, full design at `Knowledge/Designs/2026-08-05-canvas-live-surfacing-e2e-flow-and-lessons.md`. (2026-08-05, integration-audit aaf10626)

- [pitfall] **Agent has near-zero proprioception of its own UI surfaces — it produces/governs frontend state (Canvas, LeftNav cards, card Modals, SwarmWS explorer) but cannot PERCEIVE that state through the chat channel. This is an architecture-level gap, and closing it is the next focus ("给自己造本体感知").** Concretely verified 2026-08-02 against `backend/core/prompt_builder.py`: the ONLY introspection channel the agent has today is `editor_context` (prompt_builder ~line 884, "## Currently Open File") — a request-time snapshot carrying just the currently-open file's name+path. Everything else is invisible: the Canvas output list contents, pin/mute/collapsed/expanded state, whether Canvas is even open, which nav card/overlay is open, what's selected in the explorer, and — critically — the set of OPERATIONS the agent could invoke on any of these. Two consequences: (1) the agent can't answer "what am I showing the user right now / what did they just do in the UI" except for the one open file; (2) this is self-inconsistent with the Canvas mental model ("Canvas is an extension the current chat tab spouts out") — an extension whose originating body cannot sense it is not truly "grown together." The gap generalizes beyond Canvas to ALL nav-card/overlay/modal surfaces (the whole LeftNav-card + card-Modal family). Directional options (NOT yet decided): (a) **lightweight** — widen the request-time `editor_context` snapshot into a `ui_context` carrying open-file + Canvas output-count + pin/mute/collapsed + which overlay is open; reuses the existing channel, minimal change; (b) **real-time + operable** — continuous UI-state reporting (like the existing `swarm:editor-file-changed` dispatch) PLUS a declared operation surface so the agent can act on UI state mid-turn, not just read a stale snapshot. The `editor_context` request-time-only limitation (agent is blind to UI changes made DURING its own reply) is itself part of the gap to close. (2026-08-02, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **NEXT-RELEASE follow-up — prove the CI signing secret IS the 7B9C private key (assumption A, still unproven)** — The Tauri auto-updater release chain was fixed (run_5c47cf8b: release.yml now uploads updater bundles + `.sig` + generates `latest.json`) and the signing-key认知 unified (run_77ac3bee: sole authoritative key = pubkey `7B9CEDB5D3C58A4D`; orphan `E034` key pair archived reversibly to `~/.tauri/_archived-E034.key*.bak`). All 5 commits pushed to origin/main (`2ad4edf7..e6834746`). **The one thing NOT provable pre-release:** whether the GitHub CI secret `TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` actually is 7B9C's private key — GitHub secrets are write-only, so only circumstantial evidence exists (secret update date ≈ 2026-03-28, same day commit `64a917e2` switched the config pubkey to 7B9C). **WHEN THE NEXT RELEASE TAG IS CUT, do this final verification:** (1) after release CI runs, `curl` the `/releases/latest/download/latest.json` endpoint → expect **200 not 404**, and confirm the release contains `latest.json` + `SwarmAI.app.tar.gz` + `SwarmAI.app.tar.gz.sig`; (2) run `minisign` verify using the **`ED` variant** (blake2b-512 prehash — tauri v2 uses `ED`, NOT legacy `Ed`) against pubkey `7B9C` — a PASS proves the `.sig` was signed by 7B9C's private key = final proof of assumption A; (3) if **PASS** → assumption A confirmed → safe to hard-delete the archived orphan `~/.tauri/_archived-E034.key.bak` + `_archived-E034.key.pub.bak`; (4) if **FAIL** → the CI secret is NOT 7B9C's private key → rotate a fresh keypair + update BOTH config pubkey and CI secret (user-run, agent never touches secrets). **Failure mode is SAFE either way:** a bad signature makes the updater reject the update → app stays on old version, never bricks. Full verify procedure is in `s_swarm-release` Stage 7b. (2026-07-28, run_77ac3bee, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Cross-platform Rust regression class: a non-cfg-gated fn referencing a `#[cfg(target_os="macos")]`-only symbol builds fine on macOS but is a HARD E0425 on Windows/Linux — local `cargo check` (macOS) can NEVER catch it, and local cross-check is ALSO impossible (ring & other deps need a Windows C toolchain, so cross-compile dies in a native build script before reaching name-resolution)** — therefore ANY `desktop/src-tauri` Rust change that adds/moves a fn or references a platform-conditional symbol is NOT "qualified" on a green local macOS build; the ONLY real verification is CI's `build-windows` job. Instance v1.26.0 (2026-07-18): `sync_daemon_version` / `sync_daemon_version_background` / `get_daemon_version` were un-gated but called `probe_daemon_health_adaptive` + `DAEMON_UPGRADE_DRAIN_SECS` (both macOS-only) → build-windows E0425 → first release shipped with NO Windows artifacts. Fix (commit 549c3961): gate all three with `#[cfg(target_os="macos")]` (the whole daemon-upgrade path is macOS-only; its sole caller was already gated). Detection tell: grep every `#[cfg(target_os="macos")]` definition and confirm EVERY reference to it is inside equally-gated code (the adversarial pass that verified the fix did exactly this across all 6 macOS-gated symbols in lib.rs). (2026-07-18, run:release-v1.26.0)

- [decision] **The recall "0.25 crisis" was a BENCHMARK ARTIFACT, not a recall bug — proven by observation, honest production number is ~0.88** — recall_suite context_files 0.25 measured ONLY self-authored category-browse queries ("what decisions have been recorded"). Repro: 0/500 real user messages are that shape; production recall triggers on the user's first task-shaped chat message (_maybe_inject_recall→_extract_query_keywords). 10/10 hand-sampled real task queries return relevant sections; off-topic queries correctly return EMPTY (hit-bar not vacuous). Fix (run_79de25f8): recall_suite now has TWO classes — name-signal (8 browse cases KEPT to guard name-signal regression, 0.25 by design = the Gate-1-adjudicated slicer-drop) + task (8 production-shaped queries sampled VERBATIM from the transcript DB, 0.88). Number is SPLIT (context_files_by_class) so a low browse number can't masquerade as "recall broken". Lesson: before chasing a scary benchmark number, VERIFY it measures a production-real input class — a self-authored test set manufactures fake crises (C042/C044 family: I chased my own fake metric across 3 runs before verifying query shape). (2026-07-19, run_79de25f8)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **Authorship trap in a benchmark's GOLD LABELS: I reverse-tuned "intent-first" gold sets to what recall returns → task recall a vacuous 1.00; Gate-2 caught it (the exact circularity the run existed to kill)** — I made each task query's acceptable-set gold a SUPERSET of what recall surfaces (verified: returned−acceptable_union=∅), so "ANY acceptable in top-5" = grading the ranker against its own output. CLASS A at the TEST-DATA layer, reproducing the very circularity being fixed (old test's gold=probe.sections[0]). Fix: tight intent-first golds reasoned with recall NOT run, union INCLUDING sections recall MISSES (Corrections→a real 0-hit), task→honest 0.88; + structural guard test_task_golds_not_superset_of_recall_output (mutation-verified RED). Durable tell: if a benchmark's gold set is a superset of what the SUT produces, the score is circular — the gold must reach for ≥1 correct answer the system currently MISSES. (2026-07-19, run_79de25f8, Gate-2)
