- [pitfall] **Approve-into-void permission deadlock — non-atomic teardown of two lifecycle-split state pieces** (2026-07-02, run_65f317db, commit f6590cc9, ⚠️NOT deployed — needs build+restart) — A dangerous-command permission prompt's waiter coroutine cancelled (SDK `control_cancel_request`) BEFORE the user decides: `permission_manager.wait_for_permission_decision`'s `finally` pops `_pending_requests`, but `session_unit._pending_tool_use_id` stays stranded → `has_outstanding_tool_use` stuck True → session stuck WAITING_INPUT FOREVER (approve returns "Permission request not found", every new send() raises SessionBusyError, `auto_recover_waiting_input` unreachable because it needs `has_outstanding_tool_use==False`, subprocess orphaned till 12h TTL). Repro: session 831b149e / perm_01cf80ca3885. **Fix = a single dead-waiter reap predicate** — WAITING_INPUT ∧ outstanding ∧ NOT live-waiter → `force_unstick_waiting_input` (crash_to_cold clear_identity=False → --resume; `_cleanup_internal:3915` clears `_pending_tool_use_id`, self-healing the drain guard) — wired at 3 chokepoints (send() self-heal-on-next-msg; lifecycle `_check_waiting_input_timeout` ~60s tick self-heal-with-no-msg/endpoint, closing the arm the plan first missed; both approve endpoints recover-on-not-found + reset-able signal). `_has_live_outstanding_waiter()` disambiguates the correct waiter manager by `_pending_question` shape (request_id→permission, questions→ask), NOT blind-OR. Lessons: (a) Gate-0 REFUTED my original SSE-disconnect frame as WRONG-FRAME pre-code — disconnect-recovery is already triple-guarded against WAITING_INPUT (recover_from_disconnect STREAMING-only :2800, flush IDLE-only :2853, watchdog liveness excludes WAITING_INPUT :1107); (b) recovery must verify it ACTUALLY clears the stuck flag (`_cleanup_internal:3915`), not just transitions, else it's a no-op; (c) a liveness-GATED recovery is only safe if the liveness signal has NO false-negative window — meta-review proved the waiter event registers BEFORE the WAITING_INPUT transition, so a genuinely-open prompt is never reaped; (d) the reaped dangerous command is NOT silently approved (force-kill → `approve_command` never called → resume re-prompts). 8 tests, both guards mutation-verified, 113 dependency-scoped green.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active | source:manual -->

- [pitfall] **OT01 复发根因(修了 33+ 轮才找到)— 一个被 N 条分支消费的信号,只修"观察到的那一条"= 留下 silent siblings,bug 复发** — OT01("前端停 streaming 但后端还在跑 / response 不全")历史上被补丁了 33+ 次,每次都栽在同一个模式:只修了当前日志里观察到的那一条代码路径。本次终于定位真正的结构性根因 —— "后端是否还活着(post_disconnect_flushing)"这个信号被 **3 条独立的 reconcile 分支**消费(heal-grace expiry / forceClearStreamVerdict / desync-guard),而每一轮历史补丁只修了其中一条,另外两条仍然 flushing-blind → bug 换个分支继续复发。本次首次系统性全覆盖 3 条。**可迁移规则:当修一个"让信号诚实"类 bug 时,grep 出该信号的全部消费点,逐条审计 + 覆盖,绝不只修触发当前 symptom 的那一条**(PIT82 同类:sibling path 漏修)。诊断陷阱:`STORE-CLOBBER reason:"destroy"` 是红鲱鱼 —— 它只由 cleanupTabState/doCloseTab(正常关 tab)触发、全部 phase:idle,是 benign 探针,不是内容丢失的真凶;被它带偏会再次修错层。commit e7a2c011 (2026-06-29, run_0beb9e71, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **Release shipped with CI red — push 前没跑受影响测试套件** — v1.22.0 发版后 CI backend job 红 (bash_syntax_guard 的 test_zsh_brace_{function,loop}_approved)。根因: push 前只信了 origin/main 的旧绿、没在本地跑改动涉及的测试套件 —— 正是 R6 要防的"自我宣称合格"漏洞。这俩测试写死 zsh 语法假设,在 mac(zsh)上永远绿、CI Linux(bash 无 zsh)必红,本地从没暴露。修复走完整 pipeline,adversarial 否决了偏弱的 skip 方案,改成 shell-correct 断言(zsh→approve / bash→deny 双环境都验证)+ resolution-order lock。教训: release/push 前必须本地跑受影响测试套件,CI 是 push 后的正式确认、不是发现问题的地方。 (2026-06-29)

- This bug was found as a SIDE EFFECT of the adversarial in the previous run (run_c0205808). Hardening one thing (the memory_select probe) surfaced a real prod defect in the code being probed — the act of writing a rigorous guard exercises the real path and exposes what was silently broken. Guards pay dividends beyond the regression they were written for. (2026-06-27, run_edfad326, auto-cultivated)
- A bare except: pass on a block that does IMPORTS inside the try is a silent-failure factory: the import raised on EVERY call and nothing ever knew. GC19 (log the exception type) is the structural fix — and re-applying it here made the SAME class of bug immediately visible (the warning fired the moment I re-introduced the dead import). Never let an error-handling path swallow without logging. (2026-06-27, run_edfad326, auto-cultivated)
- Adversarial review surfaced a real PROD bug as a side effect (SessionRecall dead import swallowed by bare except -> recall never injected in prod). I verified it (R16b) and logged it as a tracked todo rather than fixing it inline (behavior change to a prod path = its own run) — P4 ownership without scope-creep. The bare-except that hid it is the GC19 precise-exception lesson recurring. (2026-06-27, run_c0205808, auto-cultivated)

- fail-safe means RE-DERIVE the next action, not stamp success: the deeper bug was stamping the 180s success-cooldown on failure (marked handled, no retry while context grew). Fail-safe = leave the world recoverable + retry soon (short backoff), distinct from the success cooldown. Stamp-before-await also bought re-entrancy safety (a long IDLE operation with no _transition has no mutual exclusion from its own state gate). (2026-06-27, run_37822fae, auto-cultivated)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 3 | verified: false | used: false | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->
- Fix the bug CLASS, not the instance: Gate-2 found deny had the clobber bug; the adversary then noted ask/defer share the exact class (terminal decisions, same .update overwrite). I generalized the short-circuit to deny/ask/defer in the same run rather than fixing only deny — closing the class prevents a re-run when someone adds an ask-emitting hook. allow stays out (non-terminal, carries updatedInput/additionalContext). - fail-LOUD is not fail-HARD: the fix for a silent-failure bug is observability (counter plus ERROR log), NOT changing control flow to raise. Removing the except would have violated the no-block-startup law and broken 2 callers using .strip/truthiness/len. The bug was that empty-string was SILENT and INDISTINGUISHABLE, not that empty-string was returned. (2026-06-23, run_fe0122b5, auto-cultivated)

Test the bug CLASS, not the bug instance. (2026-06-23, run_fe0122b5, auto-cultivated)

- [pitfall] **SSE-disconnect warm-reuse is the real subprocess-poisoning source — NOT user-Stop, NOT AC6 tool-budget (PIT01 trigger misattributed twice)** — Pipeline run_002eca4c self-diagnosed a stalled resume as "Same PIT01 signature — Gate 2 adversarial spawn rejected (tool-budget poisoning)". Verified FALSE against backend-daemon.log. **(1) Not AC6:** the per-turn tool-count/duration budget that called `interrupt()` was deleted in d32c3e9b (02:54) and IS in the deployed binary (d24e4614, merge-base confirmed). Zero `compaction_guard.interrupt` events after 02:54 — all 11 were 02:01–02:41, before the removal. The pipeline pattern-matched a root cause that no longer exists in running code. **(2) Not user-Stop either** (my own first hypothesis, also wrong): correlated all 7 `zombie_via_error` events today against the preceding IDLE cause — **7/7 preceded by `SSE disconnect recovery: STREAMING → IDLE`** (routers.chat), only 1/7 also had `interrupt succeeded`. The 09:08 Stop fired 22ms AFTER the IDLE transition; the 09:26 + 13:33 zombies on the same session had SSE-disconnect but NO Stop. **Real mechanism:** long turn still STREAMING → frontend SSE drops (tab switch / network / long silent agent) → routers.chat marks IDLE while keeping CLI subprocess WARM for reuse → unclean warm subprocess → next `send()` reuses it → instant (<0.01s) `result(error_during_execution, empty)` = poisoning signature. `zombie_via_error` (streaming_orchestrator.py:646) correctly catches it → kill + `--resume` respawn; 09:08 self-healed in ~12s (pid 87044 exit -9 → pid 1606 healthy through 09:14). **Signature:** zombie_via_error / error_during_execution / error_text=empty / duration<0.01s / content_emitted=False, right after a STREAMING→IDLE caused by SSE disconnect (not by interrupt). **Lesson:** PIT01's MECHANISM is correct + general; the TRIGGER attribution has now been wrong twice (AC6, then Stop). Frequency 7 zombies / 41 stops / 13 warm-interrupts = TRANSIENT, self-healed every time, never user-visible beyond ~12s. Before attributing any "poisoning" symptom to a named historical root cause, grep daemon log for the actual preceding state-transition — never pattern-match from memory (P1/COR01/GUI01/R16b). (2026-06-21)

- **2026-06-21** (session 39e315a7): **AC6 per-turn tool-count budget (=60) was a watchdog that killed normal sessions** — Root cause of the night's chat-experience collapse (send全被拒, 对话断裂, "并行 sub-agent 跑不起来"). Root-2's `_TURN_TOOL_COUNT_BUDGET=60` escalated to `interrupt()` when a single turn made ≥60 *distinct* tool calls (normal deep-research behavior). interrupt() poisoned the subprocess → every subsequent tool call (Bash, Agent dispatch) rejected by the harness. The symptom "parallel spawn rejected, single works" was MISLEADING — it wasn't a concurrency ban, it was a poisoned subprocess (single happened to hit a fresh one). Three independent failure modes were stacked (AC6 poisoning + multi-tab session desync/SESSION_BUSY + crash-resume churn) and read as one bug. Violated STEERING #1 (counted volume, not lack-of-progress; killed instead of structurally preventing). Removed entirely (commit d32c3e9b) — kept Layer-0 consecutive-repeat + Layer-1 diversity-stall detectors which judge *progress*, not *count*, so deep research never false-triggers.
  <!-- ref:52 | last:2026-06-23 | decay:active | source:manual -->

### COE10: Multi-Subsystem Untested Deploy (2026-06-17, Sev-2)

Self-Healing + Refresh Context + MessageStore single-writer shipped simultaneously via one `./prod.sh build`. All shared unverified `kill→COLD→--resume` path. 5+1 P0/P1 user-facing regressions. Root cause: flow process collapse — no independent deploy verification, no adversarial, no E2E smoke, recovery path never executed in test (`self._pid` AttributeError proves it). Fix: STEERING #12 (coupled subsystems ship independently), SWARMAI_SELF_HEAL env gate, all 5 send paths migrated to store.appendMany, resume 60s timeout. Full details: MEMORY.md [COE10].

### Patterns That Keep Failing

- [pitfall] **UI "can't click" bugs: distinguish visual occlusion from event interception** — Explorer tree badges/buttons appeared clipped (visible but unclickable). Initial diagnosis: "paddingRight too small" → increased to 20px → still broken. Root cause: ResizeHandle had a 12px invisible hit-area child (`-left-1 w-3`) at z-10, intercepting ALL mouse events on the right 12px of the tree. Fix: remove invisible hit area, use `w-2` (8px) on the handle itself. **Lesson: when user says "can't click X", first check z-index stacking context (DevTools → Layers panel), not padding/visibility.** Wasted 2 rounds on wrong diagnosis because "it looks clipped" → assumed visual cause. (2026-06-10, run_3e958d8e)

- [pitfall] **`min-h-0` must be at EVERY flex level in a nested chain, not just the leaf** — AutoSizer inside flex→flex→flex reported intermittent height=0. Adding `min-h-0` only at the AutoSizer's direct parent was insufficient — each intermediate flex container independently applies `min-height: auto` (intrinsic content size). All 3 levels needed `min-h-0` for the height to correctly propagate from the viewport to AutoSizer. Symptom: scroll works 80% of the time (when browser layout resolves in the right order) but fails on resize/transition. (2026-06-10, run_3e958d8e)

- [process] **Manual adversarial review ≠ pipeline** — Ran 2 manual adversarial sub-agents before pipeline, caught real issues (AutoSizer deadlock, WebKit compat). But skipped artifact recording, test gate, delivery convergence. Result: user deployed → still broken → another round needed. Pipeline's formal structure (REVIEW artifact → TEST gate → DELIVER convergence) would have caught the resize handle issue in round 1 because fresh sub-agent has no builder bias. Cost of skipping R1: ~40 min extra debugging + user hit a regression. (2026-06-10, run_3e958d8e)

- [pitfall] **Spinner-hang: 3 independent occurrences, same root disease — direct mutation of mutable ref state bypasses React re-render** — `tabState.isStreaming = false` (direct mutation) updates the flag but never triggers `setPendingStreamTabs` → no re-render → UI frozen on last `isStreaming=true` render. Three separate code paths had this bug: (1) disconnect timeout handler, (2) SESSION_BUSY handler, (3) compaction-guard kill. Each was independently discovered and fixed over 2 weeks. **Why hard to find:** Frontend DIAG proved events arrived, so "transport OK" → assumed "handler OK." But the handler DID run — it just mutated the flag without triggering re-render. Only checking backend `/streaming-state` endpoint (ground truth) revealed sessions were idle → bug was between flag mutation and re-render, not between event and handler. **Why kept recurring:** Plain `boolean` property on a mutable ref object — TypeScript allows `obj.prop = x` without complaint, and the code pattern looks identical to the working `setIsStreaming()` path (same assignment syntax). No compile-time signal that one path is correct and the other is broken. **Structural fix:** `readonly isStreaming` on interface (TS2540 on direct write) + `Omit<..., 'isStreaming'>` on updateTabState patch type (blocks Object.assign bypass). Now only `setIsStreaming()` can write via internal type cast. Future direct mutations are compile errors. **Diagnostic lesson:** For mutable-ref state bugs, frontend telemetry alone is insufficient — always cross-check backend ground truth. "Event arrived + handler ran" ≠ "state was correctly propagated to UI." (2026-06-07, commits 9c9ab231 + 316f8be7 + 8db685c3)
<!-- maturity: verified | sources: 3 | verified: true | used: true | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: MEMORY+EVOLUTION -->
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->
- Xueqiu API requires session cookie from homepage visit — without it all requests return 400. Adversarial caught this (HIGH) while self-review missed it. Pattern: any API that returns HTML login pages on direct access needs a cookie prefetch step. (2026-06-07, run_f2ad66a8, adversarial)
- Adversarial review on docs-only changes found 3 HIGH logic bugs including counter collision — confirms adversarial is non-negotiable even for just docs (2026-06-07, run_6215b691, auto-cultivated)
### Substantive Lessons (June–May 2026)

- [pitfall] **A doc fix that changes a recommended PATTERN needs the same failure-mode scrutiny as code** (run_88b9f986, 2026-06-26) — Fixing the publish friction (GUI03), I recommended `--quiet` always + a `$(publish --quiet | json.load stdin)` parse pattern. On a validation FAILURE `--quiet` writes the error to STDERR and leaves STDOUT EMPTY → the pipe feeds empty stdin to json.load → opaque `JSONDecodeError` with the real reason hidden. The "fix" RELOCATED the run_00e0e872 crash (verbose-output-chokes-parse → empty-stdin-chokes-parse) instead of removing it. Adversarial caught it (HIGH-1); I then hit it live twice in this same pipeline's own review+test publishes — the bug demonstrating itself. Correct fix = exit-code guard (`OUT=$(publish --quiet 2>err) || surface err`). Three reusable points: (1) a recommended CLI pattern in docs IS executable spec — review its failure mode, not just its happy path; (2) the EVALUATE here also FALSIFIED the user's stated premise (publish output was already single-line + one-pass) — reading the code reframed a "contract rewrite" into "docs + 1 additive flag" (R16b: the symptom you feel ≠ the root cause); (3) the new `schema --stage` subcommand paid for itself mid-pipeline — I dogfooded it to build the review/test/deliver payloads after failed first attempts, exactly the trial-and-error it removes.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->

- [guideline] **Streaming-render cost lives in the renderer, not the transport — gate the expensive parse on `isStreaming`** (run_00e0e872, 2026-06-26) — "Streaming feels janky on long replies" was NOT a transport/SSE/store problem (those were all confirmed per-token-immediate + rAF-coalesced). The cost was `ContentBlockRenderer:50` feeding a growing `block.text` into MarkdownRenderer (4 remark/rehype plugins + KaTeX + highlight.js) on EVERY token → O(n²) re-parse over the stream. Fix = render plaintext (`whitespace-pre-wrap`) while `isStreaming`, switch to markdown on finish (the unchanged path every historical message already uses). Three reusable points: (1) when a "feels slow" complaint hits a streaming UI, profile the RENDER leaf before the data layer — per-token full re-parse is the classic O(n²) trap; (2) the safest perf fix on a bug-sensitive frontend is a presentation-leaf conditional that reads state and writes none — blast radius is provably zero (adversarial verified a-e); (3) the cross-tab stuck-state fear (OT01/COE07-09 history) was structurally absent BECAUSE `isStreaming` is per-tab + only the re-render-triggering `setIsStreaming` writes it + a 30s reconcile loop force-clears — proving the single-writer + reconcile-backstop architecture pays off when adding new state-dependent render branches.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->

- [guideline] **After N transport-layer fixes don't hold, the bug is in the layer you never touched** (reconcile-gap, run_9db9f987, 2026-06-25) — The #1 recurring bug (COE07/08/09, ~33 patches over months) was a *truncated render after tab-switch*. Every prior fix targeted backend persistence / SSE / store-reconcile / boundary-dedup — and the bug always recurred because those layers were already innocent (store-level reconcile always returned full text). The actual defect was the React **render-source selector** (`TabView.tsx:152` dual-source `store ?? stale-prop` fallback) — a layer no prior fix had inspected. Fix = enforce ONE render source (MessageStore only; prop-fallback deleted), making divergence structurally impossible. Two meta-lessons: (1) when repeated fixes at one layer don't converge, STOP patching that layer and audit the untouched neighbor; (2) the entry was a LIVE DEV-gated repro probe, not another static patch — the bug was unreproducible at every statically-testable layer, so only live observation localized it (this was the long-standing P0 in Open Threads OT01).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->
- [guideline] **Unify scattered deciders by DELEGATION, not ABSORPTION, when the old unit is heavily tested** (RecoveryCoordinator, run_4988bfb4, 2026-06-25) — 8 independent kill-deciders unified into one RecoveryCoordinator. The blast-radius check decided the shape: HealingLoop had 1 prod caller but **5 test files** → the coordinator HOLDS HealingLoop (delegates breaker state) instead of replacing it, so all 5 test files stayed green untouched (DEC06). Owns only the DECISION (may-recover + what-kind + escalation); kill MECHANICS stay in SessionUnit. Multi-shape unification rule (PIT17): share what is TRULY universal (the enabled/user_stopped guard + the 7-verdict vocabulary), DISPATCH what differs (the gate — attempt-breaker vs cooldown vs bare-threshold vs graceful-escalation). The RecoveryTransaction lock then closed the `_crash_to_cold` TOCTOU (N concurrent callers → exactly 1 teardown) — recovery paths MUST have a forced-execution test (STEERING #11; the `self._pid` AttributeError class proved "compiles ≠ executes").
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **A self-resolving tool call swallows the real answer — fail-loud, never fail-fabricated** (AskUserQuestion block-hook, 2026-06-23) — In headless mode the CLI auto-resolves `AskUserQuestion` with an error ~19ms after emission, before the user answers; the agent then "proceeds without answer" and the real answer lands on a dead tool call. The structural fix is a `PreToolUse` gate that BLOCKS (asyncio.Event, 4h) before the CLI can self-resolve. Two sub-lessons: (1) **register the waiter synchronously BEFORE surfacing** — the enqueue↔wait gap is a lost-wakeup race (the fix for a silent-failure bug can re-introduce the same class, PIT18); (2) on timeout the gate must **DENY (re-ask), never inject an empty answer + allow** (PIT31: fail-loud ≠ fail-hard). One correlation key (`tool_use_id` on SDK `block.id`) threaded through hook → waiter → orchestrator drop-guard → SSE → endpoint → set_answer makes signal/wait pairing foolproof.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->
- [pitfall] **A "dead-end" autonomous loop is usually a SEVERED WIRE, not an unreachable threshold** (OT07 self-evolution closed loop, run_448a4f7f, 2026-06-25) — The evolution pipeline's cognitive axis "never escalated" — diagnosed for weeks as a too-high threshold. The real cause: `route_classification()` parked cognitive corrections in `governance_pending.json` and **never called `tracker.record()`**, while `escalate_class()` read only the human-verified counter (frozen at backfill). The detector and the live signal were mechanically disconnected; the counts were stale backfill, not live recurrence. Fix = wire the live signal (auto-record cognitive corrections with correction_ref dedup + 0.6 confidence floor) so escalation fires structural-fix proposals unasked at threshold 3. **Diagnostic lesson:** when an autonomous loop "doesn't fire," trace the WIRE (who calls record? who reads it?) before tuning the THRESHOLD — verify the signal reaches the detector at all (R16b: observe, don't infer). Guardrail of note: the human gate was deliberately moved to the constitution-WRITE step (git-tracked, veto-via-revert) — recording your own mistake is cognition, not a permission item — with the git-mirror as a SELF-CHOSEN check precisely because writing SOUL is the highest-confidence-lowest-self-check moment (CLASS_A = 12/0).
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant | source:manual -->

- [pitfall] **Never attempt `brew install --build-from-source` in agent sessions — compile time (10-15min) exceeds session timeout, causing unrecoverable interrupts.** Two consecutive session crashes trying to compile ffmpeg with libass. Solution: always use pre-built bottles. If a feature needs a compiled dependency that doesn't have a bottle, find an alternative approach (PIL overlay instead of libass). Rule: if `brew info <formula>` doesn't show a bottle for current platform, don't attempt install — redesign the solution. (2026-05-28, run_4a2062df)

These bugs live IN THE GAPS between files, not within any single file. Additional patterns: (1) iterative residue — Track E edited 3 times, old text not cleaned. Fix: add Cross-Deliverable Integration Review gate after multi-phase work. (2026-05-26, Pollinate v3 adversarial)

- [pitfall] **2026-04-12: Autonomous pipeline output ≠ production-ready — 20 issues in "206 tests pass" code** -- Evolution pipeline was built by autonomous pipeline (KD04, 2026-04-10). 206 unit tests, 10/10 confidence. First real E2E run exposed 9 bugs. After fixing, systematic review found 4 more structural issues. v2 redesign + PE review found 7 more. Total: 20 issues in code that "passed all tests." Root cause: autonomous pipeline excels at building components but can't verify integration. Unit tests prove parts work; only real-data E2E runs prove the system works. This is LL13's third recurrence. Rule: any autonomous pipeline output must have a real-data E2E run before merge, not just synthetic-data unit tests. (run_6fb3b0a6)

Evolution optimizer thought it modified SKILL.md when it didn't. Fix in v2: check `original in body` before replace, log warning and skip on miss. Rule: any code depending on replace() actually modifying content must verify before/after. (run_6fb3b0a6)

This class of bug is invisible in unit tests (which use tmp_path without nested .context structure). Rule: always print and assert resolved paths in integration tests. (run_6fb3b0a6)

Result: 7-day interval reset without any work done. Fix: gate state write on `not result.errors`. Pattern: any "when to run next" state must only advance when "this run actually succeeded." - [pitfall] **2026-04-08** (pipeline run_2ea8d813): **Sandbox defaults shipped with empty write paths** — `sandbox_additional_write_paths` defaulted to `""`, meaning the app's own data directory (`~/.swarm-ai/data.db`) was not writable by the agent in sandboxed mode. This broke Radar Todo and any SQLite writes. Additionally, `sandbox_excluded_commands` was missing 4 seatbelt-blocked commands (`pkill`, `top`, `screencapture`, `osascript`), and tilde paths in `sandbox_additional_write_paths` were passed to the SDK without `os.path.expanduser()`. Fix: 3 changes across 2 files, 11 new tests. Lesson: app's own data directory should always be in default write paths from day one.

- [pitfall] **2026-04-01: pytest-xdist — 12 commits, 8 days, 970 lines of conftest to solve a 3-line config problem** -- xdist kept silently falling back to serial, causing 9GB RSS OOM kills. Over 8 days, 12 commits added orphan killers, watchdog threads, background monitors, runtime `-n` injection, stale lock detection — all compensating for not putting `-n 4` in pyproject.toml addopts (the correct 1-line fix). Three interacting failure modes (not installed, not activated, agent bypass) were patched independently without mapping the causal chain. The agent itself was part of the system — it generated `-n 0` and `--override-ini` as "fixes" for xdist crashes, but nobody constrained the agent. Fix: (1) pyproject.toml addopts is single source of truth, (2) conftest.py slimmed from 970→583 lines — removed orphan killer, thread watchdog, merged preflight into worker calc, (3) conftest now overrides ALL bypass attempts (loud stderr, never silent), (4) Makefile stripped of its own `-n` logic, (5) AGENT.md rule: never pass `-n 0` or `--override-ini`. Lessons: config problems need config solutions not code; silent fallbacks are reliability's #1 enemy; the agent's behavioral patterns are failure modes; labeling code "production-grade" twice in 24 hours means you don't understand the problem yet. Deep dive: `Knowledge/Notes/2026-04-01-xdist-deep-dive-and-lessons.md`
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->
- [pitfall] **2026-03-31: Pipeline confidence 10/10 but two features dead on arrival** -- Progressive Memory Disclosure passed all 8 pipeline stages (127 tests, 0 regressions). But E2E review found: (1) `locked_write.py --regenerate-index` existed but no caller ever passed the flag — index never persisted. (2) `agent_config.get("_first_user_message")` read a key that no code ever wrote — keyword matching always got empty string. Root cause: unit tests verify components in isolation; nothing verified the caller→callee wiring. Fix: context_health_hook as catch-all for index regen, proactive briefing focus items as keyword proxy. Lesson: add "trace the wire" step to pipeline TEST stage.

- [guideline] **2026-03-31: System prompt timing blindspot** -- `build_system_prompt()` runs before user sends first message. Any feature that depends on user input at prompt-assembly time (keyword matching against first message) structurally can't work. Wrote `agent_config.get("_first_user_message")` without checking who sets it — nobody does. Lesson: when you write `.get("key")`, immediately search for who `.set()`s it.

- [pitfall] **Two credential chains cause silent failures** -- Claude CLI uses AWS SSO IdC tokens (auto-refreshed); boto3/ada uses credential_process (Isengard). Validating the wrong chain gives false negatives. Always validate the chain your code actually uses. (COE 2026-03-23, fixed `aca865b`)

## Post-Mortems (COEs)
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 1 | verified: true | used: true | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->

- [postmortem] **COE10 (2026-05-29): AskUserQuestion bilateral deadlock** — Malformed `questions: undefined` in SSE event → frontend `questions.every()` crash → React tree dies → ALL tabs dead. Simultaneously backend stuck in `WAITING_INPUT` (frontend can't answer) → session permanently unrecoverable. Root causes: (1) no runtime validation on serialization boundary (TypeScript types don't hold after SSE/DB), (2) state machine asymmetry (STREAMING had auto-recovery, WAITING_INPUT didn't), (3) no per-message error boundary. Fix: 3-layer frontend guard (`Array.isArray()` + early return + `options||[]`) + backend `force_unstick_waiting_input()` auto-recovery in `send()`. Lessons: serialization boundary = validation boundary; every non-terminal state needing external input must have force-recovery; bilateral failure = permanent (unlike unilateral which self-heals). Commit `0683fbef`. See also: `docs/post-mortems/04-bilateral-deadlock.md`.
  <!-- ref:2 | last:2026-05-29 | decay:active -->

- [pitfall] **用户 Stop 后丢内容是一条窄缺口，不走任何 content-reconcile（已知，未修）** — 核证后的判定矩阵：daemon 重启走 `backend-recovered→mergeTabFromDb`✅；网络断/SSE onerror（hasData+30s grace）走 `_postDisconnectUncertain→15s poll reconcile`✅；**用户 Stop 在 onError 撞 `userStopped` 守卫直接 return，两条都不走**❌。但严重度低：Stop 是主动 `interrupt()`（5s 内强停本轮），能丢的只有 abort→interrupt 生效之间子进程 flush 进 DB 的轮尾残块，不是整轮 response。最小正确修法是**一行**：在 `useChatStreamingLifecycle.ts:3128` 的 `userStopped` 抑制块 return 前补设 `_postDisconnectUncertain=true`，复用现成 15s poll 捞残块——**不要**加第三条独立 reconcile 路径（GUI03 多路径反模式 + 双重补发风险）。够不上"明显 bug"，等真复现"Stop 后 UI 缺一段"再动。(2026-06-21, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **`TAURI_SIGNING_PRIVATE_KEY` error at the end of a local `npm run build:all` is an ENVIRONMENT-fixed updater-signing gap, NOT a regression from the change in flight** — the `.app`/`.dmg` are produced fine; the failing step is the updater-artifact signing (needs the private key, absent in the local env). It is inherent to the local build environment and unrelated to whatever code change was just built. Do NOT chase it as a bug introduced by the current work — relaunch the freshly-built app and it runs clean. When triaging a build-tail error, first ask "is this signing/updater/env-inherent, or did my change cause it?" before diagnosing. - [pitfall] **The in-app auto-updater is NON-FUNCTIONAL end-to-end — a silent no-op, NOT protecting users (verified 2026-07-27). Do NOT assume it works.** Extends the 2026-07-18 signing-tail entry above: that one said local keyless build fails harmlessly at signing; this one is that even in CI, the signed output goes NOWHERE. The full chain is broken: (1) `release.yml`'s publish job only attaches `*.dmg`/`*-setup.exe`/`*.msi`/`*.tar.gz`/`checksums.txt` — it **never uploads `.sig` files or a `latest.json` manifest** (grep the workflow: zero updater handling); (2) `createUpdaterArtifacts:true` makes CI generate signed artifacts that are then discarded; (3) the endpoint `releases/latest/download/latest.json` → **404**; (4) the GH release is `draft:true`; (5) frontend `check()` catches the 404 → `console.warn` → idle (`UpdateNotification.tsx`), so users get NO error and NO update — silent, not a crash. **Key fork (unverified + moot):** config pubkey `7B9CEDB5D3C58A4D` vs local `~/.tauri/SwarmAI.key` `E034920AC30D40E6` are a different pair. Git timeline: `cf4caeb0` (2026-03-27) set the config pubkey to `E034…` (matching the local key); next day `64a917e2` (2026-03-28, "update pubkey to match new signing keypair") changed it to the current `7B9C…` — the local key was never rotated, so it is stranded on the OLD `E034…`. Whether the CI secret matches the config pubkey is unreadable (GitHub secrets are write-only) AND moot — nothing signed is ever published, so verification never runs. **Implication for releasers:** the DMG/exe/msi are the ONLY real delivery channel; users must manually download new versions. Making auto-update work = a deliberate separate run (fix `release.yml` to build+upload `.sig`+`latest.json`, un-draft the release, resolve the key fork) — or, if auto-update is not wanted, remove `createUpdaterArtifacts`+the updater plugin/frontend so a half-dead feature stops implying protection. Recorded, not fixed (docs-only run_6353ea42). (2026-07-27, source:manual)

- [constraint] **Sub-agent events not observable from parent stream** -- Claude SDK CLI does not emit intermediate events (tool_use, text_delta) from sub-agents to the parent stdout. When `Agent` tool is invoked, parent stream pauses entirely until tool_result returns. This is an SDK architecture constraint, not a bug. Consequence: no sub-agent progress streaming is possible without SDK changes. The elapsed timer (commit 8bf1ac13) is the maximum achievable UX improvement. Do NOT attempt sub-agent tool passthrough — it's structurally impossible at our layer.

- [pitfall] **DDD/Memory cultivation is append-only — no rewrite path (2026-06-17)** -- Cultivation engine correctly appends new lessons/decisions to safe sections (IMPROVEMENT "What Worked/Failed"). But when code changes significantly (Pipeline v3→v5), existing DDD descriptions rot because rewrite ∉ SAFE_APPEND_SECTIONS. Staleness detection works (DDD-STALE warnings fire), but detection→correction has no automated path. Distillation similarly appends new KDs to MEMORY.md but never updates stale existing KDs. - [constraint] **Desktop thinking renders EMPTY on Bedrock — upstream CLI bug, blocked, NOT our code (2026-06-21)** -- Opus 4.8 (our default model) defaults thinking `display` to `"omitted"` (empty thinking text); per Anthropic's claude-api skill the same `omitted` default applies to 4.7/Fable 5, but only 4.8 was tested this session. The fix is `display:"summarized"`, but on our Bedrock path it never reaches the API: the bundled `claude` CLI gates the thinking `display` field behind a **provider allow-list** (first-party Anthropic API, AWS-platform, Azure foundry) and **strips it for Bedrock + Vertex** before building the request. Two transports were probed — a thinking-config dict `display` key AND `extra_args["thinking-display"]` — both yield empty (CLI drops both). NOTE: deployed code uses only the `extra_args` transport (we never set a dict `display` key — see `_build_thinking_config`); the dict-display result was a probe, not the live path. **This is NOT a Bedrock platform limitation:** raw boto3 with `display:"summarized"` returns the summary fine (verified: 187 chars). **NOT anti-distillation:** the *raw* chain-of-thought is deliberately withheld on all providers (that part IS by design), but the *summary* is freely returned — Bedrock just got left off the CLI allow-list. Empirically falsified the planned one-line `_build_thinking_config` fix via pipeline run_4612261a AC1 probe: dict-display=0 chars, extra_args=0 chars, control=0 chars, boto3-direct=187 chars — all on the deployed CLI 2.1.183 AND the 2.1.185 pre-release (so "upgrade fixes it" is also falsified). Upstream tracking: **anthropics/claude-code#63358** (open, 10👍, no maintainer fix as of 2026-06-21; community root-cause by `m2de`, local patch by `Siigari` flips the minified `void 0` default to `"summarized"`). **Decision: standing pat + wait for upstream** — we keep emitting the flag (free, harmless, becomes correct the moment the CLI adds Bedrock to its allow-list, zero code change needed on our side). Rejected: (A) patching the 214MB native binary — violates STEERING #1 "structural prevention > fragile watchdog" and needs re-patching every CLI upgrade; (B) boto3-direct thinking path — architecture cost too high for "UI summary missing." Impact: cosmetic only — model reasoning quality is unaffected, only the desktop "Thinking…" panel shows empty. Docstrings in `prompt_builder.py::_build_thinking_config` + `_build_thinking_display` carry the same note.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:2026-06-21 | decay:active -->

## Security History
<!-- maturity: sparse | sources: 0 | verified: true | used: true | days: 0 | trust: high | promoted: none -->

_Notable security issues and their resolutions, so similar patterns are caught early._

- [guideline] **2026-04-15: PE review — 3 HIGH findings fixed (commit f84d8c2):** (1) Thread safety: `asyncio.to_thread` sharing module-level singletons across threads — fixed with context-manager pattern (fresh connection per call). (2) Trust boundary: subprocess commands validated for shell injection (`shell=False`) but not for tool-level injection (npm/pip/git interpret URLs, `file://`, `git+ssh://` natively) — added input validation regex for config→subprocess paths. (3) Cache canonicalization: `str(path)` used as cache key fails on symlinks — fixed with `.resolve()`. Standing rule: every `to_thread` call site gets a thread-safety audit; every config→subprocess path gets input validation.
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:dormant -->

- **R6 cross-tab eviction elimination (run_a0d93136, A→B→C, 4 commits)** — Lessons: (1) **The starting plan was stale and Gate-1 caught it twice.** A Plan sub-agent verifying line numbers against live code found R6a/R6b already shipped (the "delete compute_max_tabs" plan was a no-op); then the skeptic BLOCKED the original Step A (active-SSE registry would false-reap WAITING_INPUT sessions — SSE closes on WAITING_INPUT, answer arrives on a separate request). Both saved a wrong BUILD. Verify-against-live-code before planning is not optional on a fast-moving CRITICAL subsystem. (2) **"Delete the eviction call" was the wrong shape; "filter its candidates to orphans" was right.** Gate-1: :1004 force-evict was the SOLE anti-starvation guarantee — blind-delete leaves a hole. Filtering `_evict_idle` candidates to unowned-only preserves anti-starvation AND makes cross-tab kill structurally impossible, with call sites + control flow untouched (far safer on 140 callers). The smallest correct change beat the literal-plan change. (3) **Counter leak-proofness comes from the state-machine invariant, not vigilance.** `_streaming_count` inc/dec lives inside `_transition` because `self.state=` at :804 is the ONLY direct state write — so every exit (kill/crash/disconnect) routes through it and the counter cannot leak. Locate shared-mutable-state mutation at the single chokepoint, prove the chokepoint is unique, done. (4) **Three parallel sessions on the same CRITICAL files (R3/R6/R6b) coexisted via strangler-fig + git auto-merge** — new methods/filters only, zero existing-method edits; verified all markers present after each interleaved foreign commit (STEERING #7). (5) **Self-inflicted near-miss:** used `git checkout <file>` to undo a mutation-test edit → wiped the un-committed reaper. Caught by grep (0 markers), re-applied. NEVER `git checkout` a file holding uncommitted work — copy/restore instead (C037 class: disk ≠ intent). (6) **Two-limit split (the 3027ea6c crux) done right:** spawn_budget (existence, RAM-gated, untouched — it IS the COE05 floor) + new independent concurrent-streaming cap (peak guard). Orthogonal gates, never one conflated number. Adversarial (PUSH-READY, 0 blockers) confirmed COE05 floor intact. (2026-06-25)
  <!-- ref:0 | last:none | decay:active -->

- **Eval system decoupled from DDD via data-boundary, not code-rewrite (run_69b1c644, goal, 6 commits, 144 tests)** — XG directive: "eval should be system-level standalone, NOT depend on DDD; we just have a standard golden-case skill + extraction." TWO skeptics (EVALUATE + Gate-1) + Gate-2 each caught a different over-reach BEFORE shipping: (1) EVALUATE skeptic CUT my "v3 rewrite / kill the runtime DDD-read" framing — the gate is ALREADY decoupled by the existing `programmatic_only` flag (eval_runner.py:1154/1165 skip the LLM-judge path); the LLM judge's live-DDD-read is intentional and never gates. The decouple is at the SHIP BOUNDARY (public/private data split), not a code gut. (2) THINK skeptic killed the physical `eval/`-folder move (121 import callers, cosmetic, high-blast) → data-split-first instead. (3) Gate-1 caught the CRITICAL: the single-file write path would serialize gitignored PRIVATE cases into the tracked PUBLIC file on first auto-seed/promote — a MOD01-class leak, day 1. Fix = origin-tagged dual-file split-write (each partition re-reads + writes ITS OWN file; `_origin` default fail-closed=private; stripped on serialize). (4) Gate-2 caught C1: code_digest hashed only public golden_set but bvt counts private gate-eligible cases too → stale-green; fixed by hashing both. LESSON: when decoupling, the highest-value move is a fresh-context skeptic that READS THE CODE at EVALUATE — it found the decouple was 80% already done (programmatic_only) and that the real work was the data boundary + the write-path leak, NOT the rewrite I'd scoped. "完全解耦" delivered as: public golden_set 0 DDD refs (verified), private gitignored both repos (the live set tracks in SwarmWS not the code repo — caught mid-run), fail-closed migration (161→33 public/128 private, loss-less). The git-bound gate (code_digest binds INPUTS not HEAD; bvt green requires total>0 AND passed>0; ci_eval_gate exit 0/1/2) + s_golden-case 4-gate validator (schema/dup/non-vacuous/privacy-on-PROMOTE) make "本地跑完才放行" structural. Deferred (XG sign-off): prod.sh step0 wiring (deploy path), the 33-public promote-list review (ship boundary). (2026-06-26, run_69b1c644)

- [guideline] **Canvas move-to-ChatPage + 4-bug fix — 4 reusable tells (2026-08-02, run_99fcadb1, goal profile)**: (1) **State ownership follows the DOM position you need.** The Canvas had to sit BELOW the tab bar → it had to be owned by the component that renders the tab bar (ChatPage), not the layout. A Context-only fix (keep panel in ThreeColumnLayout) was rejected because ChatHeader is INSIDE MainChatPanel, so a layout-sibling panel is always beside the tab bar, never below it. When a UI element's required *position* is inside another component's subtree, its *state* usually belongs there too. (2) **A keep-mounted child with its OWN internal collection needs a `key` when the parent's identity changes.** FileViewer keeps an append-only internal tab list (useFileViewerTabs); without `key={activeTabId}` on FileViewerPanel it leaked tab A's file into tab B. Any long-lived child with internal accumulation + a per-context parent = add a key. (Gate-2 HIGH, would have shipped.) (3) **A proprioception/notify value pushed via a ref won't re-emit.** useCanvasHost emitted canvas-state with outputCount from a ref → the emit effect never re-fired on count change (frozen). A value that must trigger a downstream effect MUST be state, not a ref. (Gate-2 MED.) (4) **event-target (document vs window) is a silent-break contract on any listener move.** `swarm:open-file` is dispatched on `document` by 6+ sites; moving the listener to `window` would have silently broken every file-open. Gate-1 caught it pre-code; the fix was "preserve the exact target + assert it." When moving an event listener across components, grep ALL dispatchers' target first. (source:manual)
- [pitfall] **auto-surface / any "react to a live event" feature must gate on a LIVENESS signal, or historical replay re-triggers it.** Canvas auto-surface opened a stale TECH.md on every app restart because keep-mounted historical MergedToolBlocks re-dispatch swarm:file-referenced{written} on remount (component-local dedup resets). The fix: gate on active-tab `isStreaming` (the write is only "live output" during a stream) + fail CLOSED when sessionId is unresolved. Tell: if a feature reacts to an event that also fires on mount/replay, it needs a "is this happening NOW vs is this history" discriminator — mount/remount is not "now". (2026-08-02, run_99fcadb1, source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A resize/reflow-triggered "re-pin to bottom" must NOT gate on a scroll-position flag that the reflow itself corrupts (2026-08-02, run_75691aa8, Gate-2 HIGH, would have shipped self-defeating).** First cut: on ResizeObserver fire, re-pin to bottom `if (!userScrolledUp)`. But opening Canvas GROWS the content height with scrollTop unchanged → the browser fires a scroll event → the scroll handler computes `scrollTop+clientHeight < scrollHeight-threshold` = "scrolled up" = sets the guard true → suppresses the re-pin the resize was meant to do. The fix that FEELS right (guard on "is user at bottom") is defeated by the exact event it's reacting to. **Durable rule: distinguish a REFLOW-grow (scrollTop unchanged, scrollHeight changed) from a GENUINE user scroll (scrollTop moved) — only the latter should update "did the user choose to leave the bottom" intent.** A scroll event with unchanged scrollTop is layout, not intent. Extract the intent decision into a pure helper (`nextScrollIntent`) both prod and the test call — mutation-verified (neuter the scrollTop-moved guard → reflow test RED), so it's not test-theater. (source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A React `key` to force per-context state isolation is a blunt instrument — it REMOUNTS, replaying mount-animations + dropping caches/refs every context switch (2026-08-02, run_0fb40bbc).** Fixing Canvas tab-bleed, I keyed FileViewerPanel on activeTabId to give each chat tab a clean FileViewer. It worked for isolation but the E2E-from-code review caught the cost: the panel's width-reveal animation re-played on EVERY tab switch and the fetched-content cache was discarded (re-fetch flicker). **Durable rule: when a child accumulates internal state (a tab list, a cache) that must RESET on a parent-context change but the child should otherwise PERSIST (animation, cache, scroll), don't `key`-remount — pass the context id as a prop and clear ONLY the specific internal collection on change.** A `key` throws out the baby (persistent state) with the bathwater (the leak). Guards the prop approach needs: skip the undefined→first transition (don't wipe a fresh mount), and reset any path-dedup ref so the incoming context's content re-opens. (source:manual)
- [guideline] **"Build + tests green" is NOT a UX review — and jsdom cannot substitute for one (2026-08-02, run_0fb40bbc, XG pressed 3× "你端到端从用户体验上审核过了吗").** I declared Canvas "push-ready" on unit tests + adversarial code review + vite build, none of which exercise layout/scroll/animation/pixel-position (jsdom has no layout engine). A code-level E2E *trace* of the real user flows (open Canvas → switch tabs → watch reveal/spout/scroll) then caught 3 real issues (re-animate jank, cache drop, stale comment) that all green tests missed. **Rule: for a UI/UX change, "verified" requires either (a) a code-level E2E trace of each real user flow, AND (b) live visual confirmation in the running app (deploy + UI-automation screenshots) — NOT test-green alone. State explicitly which of a/b was done; never let "push-ready" imply UX-validated when only logic was tested.** The honest status ladder: code-complete < logic-tested < E2E-traced < live-UX-verified. (source:manual)

- [pitfall] **A recurring bug "fixed" N times means the FRAME is wrong — verify the runtime before patch #N+1 (2026-08-10, run_2daacd0f, 6th recurrence of Canvas-open chat-input lag; Gate-0 BLOCKED the stated root cause).** The 5 prior fixes (batch adjustHeight, contain:layout ×2 columns, field-sizing, memo) each patched a real leak point, but the bug kept coming back because nobody re-checked whether those mechanisms were even ACTIVE on this machine. Gate-0 skeptic + a runtime check proved: (a) `memo(FileViewerPanel)` already blocks the keystroke re-render — every useCanvasHost prop is referentially stable — so "lift input state down" (the fix the code comment itself deferred to) is a **no-op**; (b) **this app is Tauri/WKWebView, NOT Electron** — `sw_vers`+WebKit version showed field-sizing IS supported (Safari 17.4+ / WebKit 26), so the JS-reflow fallback the 5th fix targeted never runs here. **Durable rule: before attempting fix #N+1 on a recurring bug, VERIFY the prior fixes' mechanisms are live in the actual runtime (check the real engine — Tauri=WKWebView/Safari, not Chromium — and CSS.supports/version gates). A recurrence usually means the documented cause is already closed and the real one is unmodeled. Get the profiling/runtime evidence FIRST; a static-analysis root cause you can't reproduce is a guess.** The original pipeline brief itself said "Electron" and was wrong. (source:manual)

- [pitfall] **`contain: paint` (and `content-visibility`) makes the element a containing block for `position:FIXED` descendants, not just absolute — it will re-anchor + clip a full-screen `fixed inset-0` modal to the box (2026-08-10, run_2daacd0f, Gate-2/REVIEW HIGH, would have shipped a modal regression).** First cut added `contain:layout paint` to the Canvas content column to isolate composite cost. REVIEW found the column hosts FileEditorCore's NON-portaled `fixed` selection-comment popover (positioned via getBoundingClientRect viewport coords) + a `fixed inset-0 z-[70]` unsaved-changes modal — paint containment on an ancestor would anchor them to the column and clip the full-screen backdrop to the Canvas width. **Fix: scope paint isolation to the narrowest subtree that has NO fixed/portaled descendants** — here HtmlRenderer's root (the actual composite-heavy iframe+transform:scale surface), which only has a self-anchored absolute toolbar. **Durable rule: before adding `contain:paint`/`content-visibility` to any container, grep the subtree for `position:fixed` and non-portaled overlays/modals; paint containment silently breaks their viewport anchoring. Put the containment on the composite-heavy leaf, not a broad ancestor.** Bonus: this narrow scope is also a diagnostic — if lag persists with a CODE file (FileEditorCore) or image open (not an HTML report), the composite hypothesis is falsified and the real cause is elsewhere. (source:manual)
